    Lena finally asks for a break to go sit and have a drink a few songs in when the musician starts to play an upbeat hymn to Apollo, and their place gets taken by a group of young women almost immediately.

    “I’ve always been a little jealous of people who can believe so wholeheartedly that the gods are in control of the cosmos,” Lena says thoughtfully, watching the dancers lose themselves in revelry. Kara frowns. It’s surprising coming from Lena, who has always seemed so impervious to religion.

    “You do?”

    “It seems…easier, I suppose,” Lena explains, taking a slow sip of wine. “You can’t be terribly stressed about the future if it’s in the hands of someone else.”

    Kara shifts closer, fascinated as always with every new thought that Lena voices. “Do you struggle with thinking about the future?”

    “I struggle with knowing what I’m meant to do in this world,” Lena admits. “Knowing my purpose.”

    “So do I,” Kara agrees quietly. “I have all these powers, and I’ve always believed I was meant for something special. Maybe we both just need to figure out how to make our own way.”

    Lena leans her head on Kara’s shoulder, her furrowed brow making a return as she gets lost in thought. They sit in companionable quiet until the song changes again, and this time it’s one Kara recognizes – Lena drags her feet back to the dance floor, but soon her melancholy has faded into laughter as Kara makes an absolute fool of herself with absurd dance moves.

    The night seems to wrap up soon after with festival-goers dropping like drunken flies and the food being cleared away, and Kara is just meandering hand-in-hand with Lena through the emptying temple grounds when Lena goes white as a sheet.

    “Lena?” Kara asks, feeling the sudden resistance. Lena doesn’t speak. Her hand is squeezing Kara’s like a vise, and she’s staring at a point behind her in absolute terror. “What’s happening?”

    “Nothing,” Lena says, her voice tight. “I thought…I thought I saw someone I knew.”

    Kara’s stomach drops. She scans the crowds where Lena’s eyes are fixed, but there are so many people around – without knowing who Lena is looking for, she’s useless.

    “Where?” Kara asks, but Lena just shakes her head, looking close to tears.

    “Kara, take me home.”

    The implication of those words is much less pleasant than it was the last time Lena said them.

    Kara flies them away from Delphi the second she can find a hidden spot to take off from, flying as fast as she can while Lena shivers in her arms. She doesn’t push Lena when they’ve settled into their room, waiting for an explanation as Lena paces anxiously, but after a few minutes where Lena does nothing but wear a groove in the floor and bite at the skin around her fingers she doesn’t have much of a choice.

    “Lena, who was it? Please, just tell me.”

    Lena finally stops when Kara catches her by the wrist, pulling her down to sit side by side on the bed.

    “Otis Graves,” Lena says once she’s bitten hard enough to draw blood around her thumbnail and Kara has had to tug her hand away. “One of my brother’s more loyal allies. Lex has half the gentry in his pocket. I hoped he didn’t see my face, but…”

    “But if he did, he knows now that you aren’t dead,” Kara says, her heart sinking.

    “Yes.”

    “It’s fine,” Kara says, hoping she sounds more sure than she feels. “I’m sure he didn’t. And besides, even if he did, I flew us home. He has no way of knowing where we live.”

    Lena doesn’t seem comforted.

    Over the next weeks Lena is still mostly herself, but she’s noticeably scattered. Kara often finds her staring towards the main road, her features twisted into a frown, or wringing her hands together absent-mindedly in strange patterns that seem rooted in anxiety. Their walks and trips get less frequent and when they do happen Lena can never seem relax, constantly looking over her shoulder the way she did when she first arrived.

    Kara knows how fitfully Lena sleeps, now, and all she can really do is hold her through the long nights.

     

     

    At last, just when the cold winter weather truly sets in, it happens.

    Kara is coming back from the sea when she hears the voices. Lena mentioned yesterday that she had a craving for mussels cooked in wine for supper, and Kara decided to sneak out while she was busy helping Alex and Eliza with making preserves to gather some as a surprise – she’s still in the air when the harsh, military tones hit her ears.

    “This village is small. We know she’s hiding here.”

    “Sir, I don’t know any Luthors.”

    Kara’s blood freezes. She drops the mussels and the linen she’d been using as a makeshift bag to hold them in, and she hardly notices when they disappear into the olive trees below – the only thing that matters is getting home to Lena.

    “She’s dark of hair, pale of skin,” Kara can hear the harsh voice answer over the whistle of wind in her ears. “She’d stand out. Would have come here a few months ago, with a tall blonde woman.”

    “What do you want with her?” Kara doesn’t recognize the second voice immediately, but it sounds young – the carpenter’s son, maybe, or the boy who helps out at the olive press. The smack of hand hitting skin is loud, as is the young boy’s cry of pain.

    “That’s none of your concern. Where is she?”

    Kara hardly has time to pray that the boy will be quiet before he’s doing the opposite. He directs the harsh voice to the house down the path, their house, and Kara puts on one last burst of speed to land in the garden, leaving two deep prints in the soil with her landing.

    She finds Lena just inside the door. She’s on the floor, crying into her knees while Eliza and Alex hover nearby, not seeming to know what to do - the air feels heavy and claustrophobic, and Kara’s heart pounds harder.

    “Alex saw the soldiers in the village,” Eliza says in hushed tones. Alex is panting for breath, her brow sweaty, and more than ever before Kara loves her sister. She clearly sprinted back from the village to warn Lena to hide.

    “I’m sorry,” Lena says into her knees, her voice quavering. “I’m so sorry I brought this on you.”

    “Lena…” Kara whispers, crouching down to Lena’s level. Lena shudders, letting out a little sob, and Kara holds her close as she folds in on herself.

    “I should have known Lex wouldn’t give me up that easily,” Lena manages to say through the trembling, leaning hard into Kara. “I should have known. I got too complacent, and he – he found me.”

    “How on earth did they track us here?” Kara says, partially to Alex. “I flew us home from Delphi. Could it have been the pirates?”

    Lena shrugs, but it’s Alex that answers.

    “When you sold your things,” Alex says carefully, and with a sick feeling Kara knows she’s on the right track. “All your jewelry. Was any of it marked with your family crest?”

    Kara can feel Lena deflate.

    “Yes,” she whispers, leaning her cheek against her knee to look at Alex despondently. “My ring and hair-pin. I didn’t think – I wanted to get rid of all the ties to my old life. I wanted to help you.”

    “I know,” Kara says, rubbing her back consolingly. It doesn’t seem to help – Lena is quiet now, eerily so, with none of the usual life in her eyes. The men are approaching, Kara can hear their feet on the path that leads to the house, and the closer they get the more the panic overtakes everything else.

    “If we ignore them long enough, hide you, eventually they’ll go away,” Kara insists, wrapping her arms around Lena like she can turn them both invisible. Lena shakes her head – she’s almost limp in Kara’s arms.

    “He knows I’m alive, and that I’m here. They’ll just keep coming until I give myself up. You have no idea what this alliance is worth to him.”

    The footsteps are at their doorstep now, and Kara can hear them coming to a stop.

    “We’re looking for Lena Luthor,” a voice booms just beyond the door, and Kara has to rein in the burst of wild anger it instills in her. “Her brother Alexander knows she’s being held here, and we are authorized by his rightful rule to take her back to Athens. You have five minutes to release her from your captivity.”

    Kara breathes through her nose, squinting her x-ray vision through the door to see the men beyond. There are at least 25 of them, all armed, and the one at the head of the pack raises his fist to bang on the door three times.

    “Hiding her is an act of treason. Bring her forward, or we will have no recourse but to burn every home in this village to the ground.”

    “No,” Lena says, standing up and trying to walk towards the door. “No, no. I won’t let people lose their livelihoods for me.”

    “You can’t go!” Kara says desperately, stepping in front of the door before Lena can wrench it open. She can see flickering torchlight through the cracks. “You can’t, Lena. He can’t just take your freedom.”

    “It’s fine, okay?” Lena says, but it’s through tears so thick that they drip down her chin to leave droplets on her dress. “Thank you for everything. This has been…it’s been everything I’ve ever wanted. I’ll always be grateful for what you’ve given me. But I can’t put your family in danger. I have to accept my fate.”

    Lena reaches for the handle, but Kara holds it shut.

    “I’m going with you.”

    “You need to stay with your family,” Lena says, as if that’s somehow more important than she is. As if anything could be more important. Kara cups Lena’s chin, making sure her voice is as unshakeable as her heart.

    “Please. Please let me help.”

    “They won’t let you, Kara,” Lena argues, gesturing at the men beyond the door. “They want me alone.”

    “Watch them try to stop me. We can to go talk to your brother, and he’ll call off this wedding and his thugs, and then we can come home. Together.” Lena looks startled by Kara’s intensity, but her fear of Lex is still the stronger force – she puts a hand over Kara’s, layering them and pressing her cheek into Kara’s palm.

    “Lex doesn’t listen to anyone. He can’t be talked down. He’s Laelaps; he takes what he wants, and he disposes of anything in his way. He always wins.”

    “Lena,” Kara says firmly, stroking Lena’s jaw, “I love you. You say nothing can stop Lex, but you’re the most brilliant person I’ve ever met, and you’ve seen what I can do. If he’s Laelaps, we’re the fox.”

    “They both get turned to stone in that story,” Alex pipes in, ever the pragmatist. Her voice is tight with fear but for once she doesn’t speak up to stop Kara, doesn’t give a speech about safety or hiding, and Eliza doesn’t either. Instead she comes forward to put a hand on each of their arms.

    “When we found you outside of Delphi, we were on our way home from the oracle,” Eliza says softly, tucking a stray piece of hair behind Kara’s ear.

    “I know. I remember.”

    “What you don’t know is why we went to the oracle to begin with,” Eliza explains, and Kara frowns. She didn’t know there was a reason they went to the oracle, and she’s never thought to ask.

    “Jerimiah had been missing at sea for months, and I was - oh, I was so desperate for anything to tell me he would come home,” Eliza starts, her eyes distant. “I went to the Oracle in the hopes that she could tell me.”

    “Did she?”

    “No,” Eliza says with a sad smile. “I think she knew what I knew. That he was already gone. What she told me was that it was my fate to take in those who needed help, the most powerful of whom would have a destiny equal in happiness and strife. That she would shake foundations and birth something new from the ashes.”

    It’s an extreme prophecy even by the oracle’s standards – usually she gives little tidbits, vague allusions that can be interpreted any way the recipient is already biased towards. This is specific, and Kara can’t help but be interested despite the situation. Clearly Eliza is going somewhere with this.

    “From the ashes of what?” Kara asks, but Eliza shakes her head.

    “She didn’t say. But her riddles always make sense, in the end.”

    Kara has no idea how to interpret the information, but she files it away anyways. It seems to be giving Eliza some comfort, and if it means she won’t argue Kara staying with Lena, she’ll endure a few nonsensical prophecies.

    “I’ve told you that I’m pretty sure the oracle is just inhaling hallucinogenic fumes, right?” Kara says begrudgingly. Eliza only Eliza smiles, patting her cheek.

    “You are a gift from the gods, Kara. Nothing will ever convince me otherwise. I wanted to protect you from the strife she foretold, but I should never have tried to keep you from your path. This path.”

    Eliza pats Lena’s cheek too, making clear what she believes Kara’s destiny is. Even through her usual skepticism, Kara has to agree with that – if she has a destiny, it’s Lena. She’s not sure about this shaking foundations business, but for the most part, her path is clearer than it’s ever been.

    “When it’s safe, we’re coming home,” Kara says firmly, willing both Eliza and Lena to share the same belief. Lena still looks unsure about the whole thing; Eliza nods, and Alex pulls all three of them into a hug.

    “We’ll be here. Don’t do anything reckless when I’m not around to save your butt.”

    Alex is choked up, resisting tears with all her might, and when she pushes them away she has to wipe at her eyes. It’s shocking that Alex isn’t even protesting the idea of Kara going with Lena to Athens – she’s just accepted it as a reality. Maybe even knows how necessary it is, to protect Lena. Alex is a pragmatist, but she’s also the most fiercely loyal person Kara has ever known – Kara is infinitely grateful that that loyalty has extended to Lena, now.

    Kara gives her a gentle punch on the arm, her own eyes pricking with tears as it all really sinks in – she’s leaving. This is the only place she’s known as home since she lost her own, and she has no idea when she’ll see it again. She hopes it’s a brief separation, but in her heart she knows it can’t be that easy; there’s no guarantee of when she’ll next hug her sister.

    “Me? Reckless?” Kara says, giving Alex a grin that can’t help but be a little sad. “Never.”

    Alex rolls her watery eyes, her mouth twisted up in her attempt to keep her emotions at bay, and steps back. It feels like a final goodbye, and Kara takes it as one. She offers a hand to Lena, the other on the door handle.

    “Ready?”

    “This isn’t a good idea,” Lena says, not yet taking it. “If you get hurt because of me -”

    There’s a shout behind the door, one of the soldiers yelling at a villager by the sounds of it, and Kara feels the urgency of the moment all at once.

    “When I told you I’d be with you always, I meant it. And if I was the one in danger I think you’d do the same,” Kara says evenly, her hand staying in place. Palm-up and waiting for Lena to accept. “I don’t ever want you to suffer alone again.”

    Lena looks at it and then at the flickering torchlight behind the door, and after a last moment of deliberation she slips her fingers between Kara’s.

    The guards look up the moment the door swings open. They hardly notice Kara at all – their eyes are all on Lena, whose demeanour shifts the moment they cross the threshold. Her shoulders get stiff, her back straighter, her chin raised to look down at them figuratively and literally. Practically the whole village seems to be gathered around the house, watching the scene with everything from interest to terror.

    “I’m here of my own will,” Lena says, imperious in a way Kara has never seen. She almost looks like another person – she’s commanding, even though she’s still clutching Kara’s hand with a grip so tight that Kara is sure there’s no blood in her fingers. “Not by force. Leave these people alone.”

    The man who Kara assumes is the commander snorts, one hand resting on the hilt of his sword. His unit stands around him in a semicircle so perfect that it almost seems rehearsed. “Our orders are to return you to Athens. So long as you come with us, we have no use for these peasants.”

    The commander strides forward and reaches for Lena’s arm, and when Lena wrenches it out of his grip a dark cloud of fury passes over his face.

    “They did say that you were insolent. Lucky for me, he gave us leave to take you in by whatever means necessary,” he says, reaching to his belt to reveal a set of iron manacles.

    It’s more than Kara can take. Before he can so much as unhook them from his belt she steps forward, shielding Lena slightly with her body.

    “I’m accompanying her.”

    “She’s to come alone,” the commander growls, barely sparing Kara a second look. He unlocks the manacles, and Kara glances back to Lena for a final confirmation – can I help?

    Lena’s jaw is tight, but at Kara’s silent question she nods.

    “I don’t care,” Kara says more confidently, looking back at the armoured man with Lena’s go-ahead. “If you want her, you get me.”

    “This is your only warning, malaka,” the commander shouts, his temper breaking now at her interference. “Step aside.”

    He lunges towards them, manacles at the ready, but he’s almost sent sprawling when he bounces off the immovable wall of Kara’s body. The cuffs fly from his grip to land in the dust near Kara’s feet.

    “And this is your only warning,” Kara says, snatching them up before he can get the chance and tossing them easily into the dark fields beyond the fence. “Lena is under my protection.”

    “Enough of this,” the commander grunts, clearly thrown off by Kara’s strength but not yet willing to admit it. He turns to his men, raising his voice to give orders. “Raze the place, and take the girl alive. I don’t care what you do with the rest.”

    One soldier reaches an arm back obediently to lob a torch at the nearest field, and Kara reacts without a second thought. The torch sputters and dies with a blast of her freeze breath before it even hits the ground, and everyone in the vicinity – soldiers, villagers, even the commander – ducks down in fear at the wave of impossible frost it unleashes. The soldiers cower, staring at Kara in wonder and terror, and it’s upon seeing his men lose their courage that the commander finally loses his head. He unsheathes his sword, swinging it over his head in a wide arc aiming to come down on Kara’s torso, and somewhere behind her Lena lets out a shout of fear.

    When Kara catches the blade in her bare hand, her eyes glowing with furious fire at the man who thought he could separate them, she can almost pinpoint the exact moment when he loses control of his bladder.

    The crowd, beginning to feel more like an audience, erupts into gasps and whispers. Each and every one of them takes a few steps back, and Kara can hear their exclamations to each other as they stare at the unexplainable scene before them.

    “The gods are among us.”

    “That can’t be Kara, can it?”

    “Her eyes…”

    The villagers, Kara’s friends, the people who have watched her grow from a scared child to the person she is now, are all looking at her like they’ve never seen her before. There’s awe and disbelief, wonder, relief at being spared the soldiers’ wrath; but worst of all, Kara sees a palpable undercurrent of fear.

    They’re afraid of her.

    She wants to explain. She wants to tell them no, no, she isn’t a god – this whole puppet show is just the only way she can save the woman she loves. She’s the same Kara she’s always been, the person they’ve all trusted to help them with labour or watch their children. But that would defeat the purpose of what she’s doing entirely. For this to work, she has to let them fear her. She has to revoke who she used to be.

    In all her years dreaming of this moment, she never once thought that she would feel so sad.

    The commander drops to his knees. He’s still holding the handle of the sword, and his arm wrenches upwards when Kara holds the blade in place.

    “Lady Athena,” he gasps, staring up at her in reverence and dread.

    “Not Athena,” Kara counters quickly, swallowing hard. She needs to appear in control. Powerful. Gods and demigods don’t show weakness, and she understands now the part she has to play to get Lena out of this unscathed. She can’t show even a shadow of doubt in herself. “But her kin. She guides my path.”

    In an easy movement Kara bends the blade in two and twists it into a loop, tossing it aside. Two nearby soldiers scatter to avoid where it lands.

    “We are servants of your sacred city. What do you ask of us?” the commander asks, bowing his head. There are beads of sweat dripping down his temples, and his supplication makes Kara’s stomach turn.

    “I’m taking Lena to Athens,” Kara announces, directing it not to him but to the rest of his unit. “Leave this place, and go back to your master. If you ever return, if you ever harm these people again, you now know whose wrath you would incur.”

    With her intentions stated Kara finally turns back to Lena, the only person left not afraid of her. There’s fear in her face, but it’s not of Kara’s powers – it’s of the implications of what she’s just done.

    “Kara,” Lena breathes when Kara steps into her embrace, her voice trembling as she wraps her arms around Kara’s shoulders. “Those men won’t keep it to themselves. There’s no going back from this.”

    “It’s okay,” Kara whispers into her dark hair, hoping that if she says it enough it might become true in the short travel time between here and Athens. “As long as we’re together, it’s okay. We can figure it out.”

    She gathers Lena up securely for the flight, and making sure she’s in full view of the commander and his men she shoots into the sky, leaving home behind.

    Chapter End Notes

    OOOH BOY be a shame if there was any machinations when they get to Athens, huh?

    Agora – the central marketplace in most cities
    Frieze/metope – decorative elements on a temple, up high above the top of the columns and brightly painted
    Ionia – the area around the coast of modern-day Turkey, contained lots of older Greek colonies which later were annexed by Persia
    Satrap – Persian city administrator
    Tyrant – ruler of a Greek city who took power rather than inheriting it by birth, though it didn’t have the same negative connotations we think of now. Generally just meant a ruler who wasn’t a king or elected official
    Malaka – Greek swear word ;)

    Chapter 3

    Chapter Notes

    I last updated this in...uhh....August???? Big yikes lmao

    Sorry for the long wait! Had to get my creative juices back. And the wait for the finale will be MUCH shorter, as it's already almost finished. Hope you guys enjoy!

    The situation truly begins to sink in the further Kara flies from home.

    She makes towards Athens as quickly as she can without making the journey too uncomfortable for Lena. The cool wind whistles around them, Lena’s face tucked into her neck, for long minutes as Kara flies east towards the last moments of sunset - the dying light is just enough for Kara to see the distant shape of the great city rising in hills above the countryside around it, a larger swath of civilization than Kara has ever seen on this planet. Larger than Megara, larger even than Ephesus even if it’s not quite so grand. Practically a metropolis by the standards of Greece.

    Before they can get too close, Lena tugs on Kara’s shoulder to get her attention.

    “Can we land?” Lena calls over the wind. “Outside the city?”

    With a quick nod Kara touches down in a patch of scrubby trees just outside the city gates, gingerly setting Lena down, and immediately Lena starts to pace back and forth.

    “What is it?” Kara asks, following Lena’s path with her eyes.

    “We need to talk before we get to Athens,” Lena says, distracted enough that she doesn’t bother to fuss with her windblown hair.

    “My brother…he won’t bow to you like those others,” Lena says as she paces. Her peplos is swirling around her feet, the hem continually getting caught under her sandals. “He claims piety to keep the people in his favour but he believes in the gods as little as I do. This Athena charade won’t work on him.”

    Kara frowns. “Even if he doesn’t think I’m divine, he has to understand what I can do. We’ll show him my abilities.”

    “He understands power,” Lena argues. “And how to keep it. He and my stepmother overthrew the last tyrant of Athens to get his position – he won’t give me up easily when I can still be useful to him.”

    Kara nods but Lena continues to pace, her hands wringing nervously together.

    “We need to be careful. Both of us.”

    “Okay. I’ll take your lead,” Kara says. Lena doesn’t seem assured. When she finally comes to a stop in front of Kara she’s even paler than usual.

    “Kara, I’m going to be…” Lena sighs, crossing her arms tightly over her chest. “Things will be different in Athens. I’ll be different. I have to be. I survived here my whole life by wearing a mask, and I need to put it on again to get us through this.” The perfect lines of Lena’s face, so familiar to Kara after almost a year of tracing them in loving detail with her eyes and hands, are deeper-set than usual. All Kara can do is nod again.

    “Of course. Whatever you need to do.”

    “I need you to know that how I feel about you hasn’t changed,” Lena says softly, coming forward a few steps and taking Kara’s hands. “Won’t ever change. But Lex will find everything he can to use against me, including my love for you. I can’t let him know how deep my feelings run.” She kisses each of Kara’s knuckles until Kara stands up, pulling her close and holding her as tightly as she can without it being painful.

    “I understand,” Kara murmurs into her hair. She doesn’t quite know what to expect, but whatever Lena needs to do to get them out of Athens and back home will be worth it in the end.

    Only after a few minutes spent looking at the city walls with an air of deep resentment does Lena allow Kara to bundle her up and take flight again, and soon they’re touching down in the last of the day’s bluish light within eyesight of a group of guards on duty outside the city’s south gate. They all stare as Kara lands dramatically with Lena in her arms, mouths agape, and she sets Lena back on her feet as delicately as she can.

    “My lady,” the guard with the largest helmet says when they’re at a close enough distance for Lena to be recognized, giving a small bow, but his eyes stay glued on Kara. “The tyrannos said that your retinue was not yet due for a few weeks.”

    Lena’s face is a stone mask. “Clearly I’m not with the retinue. Take me to my brother.”

    Her voice is haughty and severe, sharp as a blade, and the guards jump to follow her demand faster than Kara has ever seen a soldier obey a woman. Two of them lead the way through the squalid outer neighborhoods and towards the central hill, and after a long, steadily darkening walk through unfamiliar streets the buildings around them begin to increase in size and wealth until they’re just below the shadow of what Kara assumes is the city’s acropolis. She can’t see far enough up in the dark, but the street in front of the massive, opulent home the guards leave them in front of is well-lit with torches.

    “Is this your house?” Kara whispers. It’s more of a palace, really, a sprawling estate with a bright terracotta roof covering multiple floors, and she can see gardens beyond the house larger than any she’s ever seen within the walls of a city.

    Lena nods tightly.

    “It used to be. Follow me, and let me do the talking.”

    With one last squeeze of Kara’s hand Lena strides through the doors, and Kara follows with her mouth shut.

    Three servants in matching green robes meet them just inside the entryway. They all seem shocked to see Lena, but none more than the one standing in the middle – though she schools her expression quickly, the wonder and relief upon seeing Lena is obvious. Kara can’t tell if Lena makes eye contact with her – the servant averts her eyes quickly, and Lena passes the group by wordlessly. She clearly knows exactly where she’s going, and Kara trails her through hallways hung with colourful wall paintings and woven tapestries until they emerge into what Kara can only describe as a banqueting hall.

    It’s a massive, warm room, open to the heavens and lit by torches and a well-maintained fire pit. It’s centred around a long, low table overflowing with an amount of food and wine that far exceeds the needs of the two figures seated at it. One is a woman, about Eliza’s age at a glance but with none of her warmth or friendliness – in fact the moment she sees Lena her face turns frosty, her hand gripping harder at her beautifully-painted wine cup. The other figure is, Kara can only assume, Lena’s brother.

    Lex is nothing like what Kara had imagined. Lena built him up so much as a danger to them that Kara had been imagining someone imposing, someone physically threatening in some way, but the first word that comes to mind when he rises up from his couch and throws his arms wide in welcome is small. He’s hardly Kara’s height, perhaps even a bit shorter, with narrow shoulders that aren’t quite hidden by his robe and vibrant purple silk chlamys. He’s also completely bald, though he has a short-trimmed beard to compensate for the lack of hair on his head, and his smile is wide and magnanimous. Friendly on the outside, but something about it sends a shiver down Kara’s spine.

    “Adelphé! How overjoyed I am to see you alive and well!” he booms with all the charm and practice of a seasoned orator. The woman at his side stares silently at Kara over the rim of her wine cup, her eyes narrowed, but Lex steps around the table to pull Lena into what looks like a deeply uncomfortable hug. “I was worried sick when I received news of your shipwreck.”

    “Yes, I’m sure Morgan was heartbroken not to have the wife he paid good coin for,” Lena says, tense in his arms until he finally releases her. Lex only chuckles and pats her on the arm as if Lena is a child throwing a minor tantrum.

    “Now, now, what did I tell you about pouting? We all have our parts to play.” His gaze turns to Kara, looking her up and down with an interest that makes her skin crawl. “And who is this?”

    “Kara,” Lena says. There’s no inflection behind the word, not so much as a hint of the affection with which Lena usually says her name, but Lex’s eyes are still bright and entirely too interested. “Her family has been hosting me.”

    “I heard your captor was formidable, but I wasn’t quite expecting this beast,” Lex chuckles, walking in a wide circle around Kara to take in every angle. He is, as it turns out, almost a full hand shorter than she is when he isn’t standing on a dais. Kara stays perfectly still with her jaw clenched tight, fighting to keep quiet. “And you allowed her to deliver you to me?”

    “She isn’t my captor,” Lena says sharply. “And I think you know that. I’ve never wanted a part in your schemes. Let me go home.”

    “You are home,” the woman at the table says. Kara can’t be sure, but it’s an easy assumption to make that this is Lena’s stepmother. Lillian. The one whose injustices Lena has told Kara countless stories about through tears in the dark privacy of their room, whose harsh treatment of Lena throughout her childhood left a mark that still hasn’t faded. Her voice is arrogant and condescending, and though Lena doesn’t flinch Kara can sense her agitation as soon as her stepmother opens her mouth.

    “And yet you two sent me away. I’ve found a new home, now,” Lena says, disregarding Lillian and turning instead to her brother. “Please, Lex. If there’s any shred of the man you used to be left inside you, set me free. I never wanted any of this.”

    Lex doesn’t react. There’s not a flicker of empathy behind his eyes, no hint of mercy – just the same empty smile. “And if I don’t?”

    Kara’s fists clench ever tighter. She’d love to show him exactly what will happen if he doesn’t, and thankfully Lena’s cue for her comes soon - she sighs. Her disappointment is clear, but she looks to Kara and gives her a brief nod.

    Summoning all the anger she’s feeling on Lena’s behalf, Kara lets her eyes glow with furious fire.

    “Oh,” Lex says, his face eerily illuminated, and just as Lena claimed there’s no true fear in his eyes. Not even surprise, which is almost more unsettling. Just fascination, and an off-putting sort of greed. “Oh, now this is interesting. Wherever did you find her, Lena?”

    “She found me,” Lena says shortly. “You can see that stopping her isn’t an option. Will you let me go?”

    Lex is silent for a time. His gaze is sharp, analytical, like a coiled snake looking for the best time to strike, until his smile turns overly friendly and he opens his arms again, gesturing at the table.

    “Sit, sister. Share supper with us,” he says, going back to his couch with the clear assumption that Lena will obey. “I need to get to know your companion before I just send you off to frolic in the countryside.”

    Lena looks like she wants to refuse, but ultimately she takes a seat at the opposite end of the table. Kara follows her lead. As soon as they’re seated two servants appear to pile their plates with food before disappearing again - Kara wonders, as they slip into the dark corners of the room, if Lex ever does anything for himself. As servants fill his plate and wine cup, throw away his refuse, and wipe his mouth. It’s grotesque.

    “So. Kara,” Lex says, tearing a leg from a whole roasted rabbit and biting into it to address her with his mouth full. “Where do you hail from?”

    “The village your soldiers were sent to. Near Megara,” Kara says, trying to keep the venom out of her voice. Lex narrows his eyes, understanding the feint, but his smile stays fixed.

    “And these powers of yours. These eyes of fire. What others do you have?”

    “Flight. Strength,” Kara says vaguely, leaving out the less quantifiable ones. She’d rather Lex not have a full report on everything she can do. “Enough to keep Lena safe.”

    Lex laughs, tossing his half-finished rabbit leg aside to wash it down by draining his wine-cup. He snaps his fingers, and it’s re-filled immediately. “Oh, Lena has always been quite capable of taking care of herself.”

    Taking care of herself against what? Kara wants to ask. Against you? But she doesn’t. She stays quiet, speaking only when spoken to, and Lena seems grateful.

    Kara eats as they talk, giving half-lies mixed with the truth to Lex’s endless questions between bites, but Lena doesn’t touch the food. They’re offered expensive cuts of roasted meats, eels poached in mulberry sauce, cucumbers in yogurt, and kykeion to drink - there’s even dessert, fruit and fluffy little loukmades soaked in honey syrup and sesame seeds. All foods Kara has never tasted, and more than Kara has ever been given in a single sitting. Enough to fully sate her, for once. The wine is strong and heavily spiced, and over endless cups of it Lillian stares daggers at Kara across the table.

    Finally Lex seems to tire of questioning Kara and turns his attention to Lena instead, allowing a servant to clean his hands with a damp cloth before folding his fingers together on the table.

    “Do you know why I was sending you off to be married, Lena?” he asks. Lena answers in a monotone.

    “An alliance with Corinth.”

    “Yes, but why? Why would I need such a thing?” His tone is insufferably patronizing. He’s quizzing her, and Kara can tell from this interaction alone that this has been common all Lena’s life. Making Lena prove herself to him over and over, never allowing her to measure up to whatever mysterious standard he’s holding her to.

    Lena’s jaw flexes. “Persia.”

    “Exactly,” Lex hisses, his grin almost macabre. “Persia. An invasion from the East is inevitable, and I need Corinth’s armies to fight it.”

    “Why not make an alliance with Sparta if you’re looking for military strength?” Kara says, forgetting for a moment that she’s meant to be quiet. “Why Corinth?”

    Lex’s face flashes with irritation. “Sparta’s kings are stubborn. They refuse to bow to an Athenian. But they will.”

    There’s something ominous about his wording, and Lena’s body language changes. She leans forward, her brow furrowing. “Bow? Lex…is your intention to attack Sparta?”

    “My intention is to save Greece from becoming a Persian fiefdom,” Lex spits, his face steadily reddening. “They’ve already taken Macedon as a vassal. They’ve had Ionia for two generations. They press on our heartland more each year, and yet all cities but mine seem content to wait until they’re at the doorstep.”

    “So you take each city-state for yourself instead?” Lena scoffs. “Are you insane?”

    “Only a united Empire led by a strong hand can defend against another,” Lex argues. He’s clearly unhappy at Lena’s pushing, but Lena is so incredulous at his boldfaced audacity that she doesn’t let up.

    “And you’ll be the king of this twisted Empire, will you?”

    “The Persian armies drink rivers dry. They trample fields to raw earth,” Lex says. Lillian is nodding, agreeing with his every word like he’s a priest giving a morning prayer. “They have soldiers a hundred times what a single city-state could muster. Who could stand against them but me?”

    Lena scoffs. “Your self-importance knows no bounds.”

    “If Greece doe not unite under me, it will crumble under the Persian yoke. I will not yield Athens to barbarian invaders,” Lex says loudly. Lillian intercedes then, her voice much quieter but equally insidious.

    “War and politics are outside your narrow scope, dear. It’s best to just accept your role,” Lillian says smugly, smiling at Lena. “To marry Morgan Edge. Bear him healthy sons, and solidify the alliance with Corinth for your brother. Tomorrow we’ll charter another ship for you.”

    Lillian looks terribly proud of Lex for his machinations, but he doesn’t return the sentiment. He slams his hand down on the table, rattling dishes and cups and spilling wine over the fine white cloth draped over the wood.

    “Do not deign to speak for me, mother!” Lex shouts. Lillian doesn’t flinch, but she quiets at his word. There’s a mania in his bearing that makes Kara nervous, even though he calms down after his outburst and goes back to his unsettling smiling. “Haven’t you been listening? Lena doesn’t want to go to Corinth. We should give her the chance to show us that she can be useful in another way. Perhaps then she can retreat to her new…pastoral lifestyle.”

    Lena sighs. “What do you want from me, Lex?”

    “A discussion for another day,” Lex says cheerfully, rounding the table. “Come – your old rooms are ready for you. You can change out of these dreadful clothes and make yourself comfortable.” He plucks at the garment Lena made herself, sharing a titter with Lillian, and it takes all the willpower Kara has not to turn him into a roast chicken right then and there. As if he can feel her ire he turns to Kara with that same infuriating smile.

    “Your companion can stay in the guest wing,” he says, a bit too knowingly for Kara’s tastes. “We’ll talk in the morning.”

    Lex snaps his fingers, and with no delay his servants start to clear the table as he strides purposefully out of the room. Lillian and Lena follow, and with only a final glance from Lena to comfort her Kara is led down the opposite hallway.

    The guest room she’s shown to is more lavish than anywhere she’s slept since Krypton – a large bed, painted walls hung in rich fabrics, a small stone basin for washing, and a bowl of perfumed oil and flower petals on the table scenting the space with a heavy fragrance - but the moment the door closes behind her she can feel the physical distance between herself and Lena like they’re on opposite sides of an isthmus. It feels wrong. She’s slept beside Lena for months now, been the one to kiss her goodnight and hold her through her night terrors, and now that they’re in the very place those terrors stem from it feels deeply wrong to be apart. So, once she’s settled in, Kara bucks the rules.

    She follows Lena’s heartbeat to the other side of the estate, using strategic bursts of superspeed to avoid being seen until she can hear Lena breathing on the other side of a heavy door. She slips past it, letting it close gently behind her. Lena is sitting at a vanity table when Kara enters, staring at her own reflection in a polished copper mirror; she looks up at the noise of the door opening, her hand grasping for something in a drawer, but when she sees that Kara is the intruder she pulls her hand from the drawer and relaxes.

    The clatter of a knife hitting wood is distinct enough for Kara to recognize it.

    “Kara,” Lena breathes, rising and immediately sinking into Kara’s arms. Her words hold a warning, but her body feels like reprieve. “You shouldn’t be here. If someone sees, and tells Lex -”

    “I know. I won’t stay. I just needed to see you.” For a moment Kara just holds her, Lena’s face tucked against her chest, soaking in the only contact she’s going to get for tonight. “So much for leaving right away, huh?”

    Lena sighs. It ruffles Kara’s hair, a soul-deep exhale of pure exhaustion. “I told you he was a conniver. But even I had no idea how deep his fixation ran.”

    “He’s just as paranoid as you said he was. All this fuss over Persia?” Kara says, kissing the top of Lena’s head. “Do you think there’s any truth behind it?”

    “They have been expanding aggressively,” Lena says. She sounds so weary, so tired already by the charade this place forces her into. Kara misses her easy smiles from home. Her quick laugh and the dimples it brings to her cheeks. “A war is becoming inevitable. But he’s wrong about how to stop it. People will fight for their own freedom much harder than for a ruler they didn’t choose.”

    Lena sighs again, burrowing deeper into Kara’s embrace.

    “All this talk of war and politics and I just want to run home with you and leave it all behind again,” she murmurs, pressing a kiss to Kara’s chest. “Maybe I’m just as useless as Lillian says. Meant to be used as a tool.”

    Kara frowns. “Lena, no -”

    Voices echo down the hallway. Footsteps are rapidly approaching, and Lena pulls away before Kara can protest her self-judgement. Her arms wrap around herself, a protective barrier that not even Kara can ease. “I’m glad you came, Kara, but you need to go. If someone hears you here…”

    Kara nods, swallowing thickly. “Okay. I’ll see you in the morning?”

    Lena nods. Her eyes are shiny, but no tears spill over. Kara wonders how long it’s been since she allowed herself to cry in this place without fearing it would show weakness to her maniac brother. “Lex breaks his fast later in the morning. A servant will come to bring you to the table.”

    Kara nods again. She doesn’t trust herself to speak without either crying or begging Lena to leave with her; instead she goes back to her room, speeding through the halls, and without Lena beside her she tosses and turns all night. Lena’s heartbeat, usually the soothing sound that lulls her to sleep, never slows down to restfulness.

     

     

    As Lena implied, breakfast here doesn’t begin until well after sun-up. Kara, being used to eating before the sky has lightened, is absolutely starving by the time someone comes to fetch her and lead her to the dining-room, and she’s relieved to see that just like last night the table is absurdly overladen with food. Decorated dishes are spilling over with fresh fruit and porridge, honey-drizzled yellow griddle cakes with walnuts and delicate little quail’s eggs, rather than the plain barley bread and wine she’s used to breaking her fast with; but more importantly Lena is waiting at the table. Lex and Lillian haven’t arrived yet, and Kara’s desire for food is overridden. Lena looks just as tired as Kara feels, and she shoots Kara a small smile.

    “Good morning,” Kara says quietly. Lena’s eyes are hooded, dark circles just visible underneath, but she puts on a brave face.

    “Morning. You must be starving. Sit.”

    “I am. It’s practically lunchtime,” Kara grumbles, taking a heavy seat across the table from Lena. “Am I allowed to eat before he graces us with his presence, or…?”

    Lena chuckles. “You can eat. Try the teganitai.”

    “The what?”

    Lena points to the plate piled high with small flat cakes. Kara grabs a few, stuffing one whole into her mouth and then pausing when the most incredible flavour registers on her tongue. The texture is light and fluffy, so different from the barley-cakes Kara knows, and the honey syrup has soaked through the crisp exterior to permeate the whole thing with a gentle sweetness that ends in a perfect crunch from the toasty walnuts sprinkled on the top.

    Kara moans mid-bite.

    “I thought you might like them,” Lena says, her smile openly fond for the first time since they left the village last night. Like just for a moment, she’s forgotten where they are.

    “They’re incredible. What are they made of?”

    “Wheat flour.”

    Kara eats another, but before she can try to prolong Lena’s small moment of happiness Lex strides in. His robe billows behind him, all pomp and self-importance, and Lena’s smile dies on her lips.

    Kara hates him more than ever before.

    “Good morning, all! I trust you slept well? I’m sure our beds are far more comfortable than whatever you’re used to,” Lex chuckles as he takes his seat at the head of the table, and Kara grits her teeth so hard her gums start to ache.

    “Fine,” Kara says shortly, looking to Lena. Lena has put a single apricot and a handful of almonds on her plate, but doesn’t touch it any further.

    “As well as ever,” Lena says enigmatically. It makes Kara unthinkably sad to think that Lena’s sleep last night was indicative of how she’s always slept here.

    “Lovely! Dig in,” Lex says, just as Lillian appears flanked by servants. “Fruits from the Peloponnese, honey from our very own apiary. And the wine, it’s just in from Cyrene!”

    The food is delicious, as much as the taste is marred by Lex’s boasting about its exotic origins and Lillian’s continual cold glares, but Kara’s appetite wanes the longer the meal goes on without addressing last night’s question.

    When can we go home?

    She tries to bring it up once or twice, but stubbornly Lex still refuses to engage in the only conversation Kara wants to have. He insists again that it’s a topic for later, that he has a busy day of meetings and petitions from his people, and after a few cups of wine and not much food he disappears with Lillian behind him, having said nothing of consequence at all.

    The only truly good thing about breakfast is that Lex is occupied afterwards, and once he’s left Lena takes Kara for a walk around the estate’s gardens where they can actually talk.

    “What if we just go?” Kara says, trying to keep her voice low. “I know you said it wouldn’t work, but -”

    Lena shakes her head heavily. “We’d never be safe. Nor would Eliza and Alex.”

    “I could fly us all somewhere far away. Anywhere in the world. He can’t cross oceans. I can.”

    “And take your family away from their home?” Lena counters. The gardens are full of unfamiliar plants, taken from distant foreign shores and forced to grow in Greek soil. Some look to be thriving an lush with fruit and fragrant flowers but others are wilted and brown, craving the environment they’re accustomed to. “And what about all the people he’d hurt in his search for me, or in this deranged quest he’s given himself? The people in the village? He’d burn it to the ground out of spite the second we left.”

    Kara sighs, kicking at the dusty ground with her sandal. “You’re right. I know. But…why? Why would he go to such lengths?”

    “To him I’m a possession,” Lena explains, something deadened and heavy in her voice. “A game piece that he needs to fulfill whatever his ultimate plan is. And he doesn’t like his toys being taken away.”

    It’s an ominous statement, and one that sits like a rock in Kara’s chest even when Lena’s attention turns elsewhere.

    “Lena!”

    An unfamiliar voice cries it from across the gardens, and Kara whirls around at the same time Lena does to see three figures rushing across the grounds towards them. Kara doesn’t recognize them from the small handful of people she’s seen around the house, but Lena’s face breaks into a wide smile as the dark-skinned woman leading the pack meets her with a long, tight hug.

    “It is you!” the woman says, laughing with delight. “The messenger was right – he said you’d arrived with another demigod in tow. I hardly dared to believe.”

    Kara frowns.

    Another demigod? It’s a concerning statement, but nobody else seems to address it.

    “Kelly,” Lena says with great affection, pulling back from the hug only to receive two more from the men accompanying her friend. “Oh, it’s good to see you. All of you.”

    “Thank the gods. We feared you were lost for good,” one of the men says solemnly. He’s tall, taller than Kara certainly, with the darkest skin Kara has ever seen and a serious sort of face. The second man, much shorter and more compact with his long dark hair tied back, hugs Lena the tightest of the three.

    “It is good to see you alive and well,” he says warmly. “The way your brother told it, you were kidnapped by pirates! Quite a romantic tale.”

    “You have no idea,” Lena says, sharing a knowing smile with him. “I’d probably have been sold off in Chios if not for Kara.”

    Lena puts a hand on Kara’s arm and all three of her friends look to Kara with varying degrees of reverence, not seeming sure how to address her. Kara gives an awkward wave.

    “Hi,” she says weakly.

    “Introductions would probably be helpful,” Lena says, her hand resting near Kara’s elbow. The contact is a balm.

    “J’onn is a magistrate. He helps to deal with the city’s finances,” Lena explains, gesturing to the taller man, and J’onn smiles. It breaks the graveness of his disposition into something more fatherly, and Kara begins to warm to him before Lena moves on to the second man. “And Brainy is a playwright.”

    “Purveyor of the arts and all things dramatic,” Brainy says in an orator’s voice, giving a steep bow in Kara’s direction. “Though my creativity has been somewhat stifled of late. Art thrives on parody of the powerful, and Lex does not appreciate being the butt of the joke.”

    “Brainy’s plays have turned many a heart and mind against Lex’s rule,” Lena explains. Brainy smiles indulgently.

    “Lena does me too much credit.”

    “And of course, this is Kelly,” Lena says, her other hand going to Kelly’s shoulder. They’re close, Kara can tell, and Kelly has a warmth to her that Kara finds comfortable immediately.

    “A lowly metic,” Kelly says, smiling. “A transplant from Cyrene of no import.”

    “Don’t be silly.” J’onn waves the idea away. “You and your brother are integral to the cause. Your foreign contacts are invaluable.”

    “Where is James?” Lena asks, frowning around the gardens like she’s expecting this fourth person to appear. Kelly sighs.

    “Sent off with his cohort. Some silly scouting mission for Lex. He’s due back in a few weeks.”

    “James is an officer,” Lena explains for Kara’s benefit. “And an absolute genius in the realm of military strategy. Not that Lex ever valued his input.”

    “It’s nice to meet you all,” Kara says.

    “They’re my dearest friends,” Lena says. Her smile is undeniably fond, but there’s something else there as well that Kara can’t quite pin down. Lena’s friends don’t give Kara much time to contemplate it – they move swiftly on to the crux of things.

    “Dearest friends, and co-conspirators,” J’onn says, lowering his already-deep voice. “Without Lena, we feared our cause was lost. We must give thanks to the gods for their divine intervention.” He bows to Kara again, and Kara tries hard not to wince.

    Barely a day into this god-charade, and already she detests it.

    She catches Lena’s eye, a silent question, and Lena gives her a little nod. A confirmation. These people are safe.

    “I’m not a god,” Kara says, already relieved to have a small group of people around whom she won’t have to weather constant adulation. “I do have powers, but they aren’t divine. It’s…difficult to explain. But it’s important to know that I’m just a person. I’m not…you know. Associated with Athena. I can’t talk to Zeus or anything.”

    They all look to Lena, clearly confused, and Lena nods in agreement. “She’s telling the truth.”

    Kelly shares a significant look with J’onn and Brainy.

    “What?” Lena says, narrowing her eyes. “What was that look?”

    “A man arrived not long after you were sent away,” Kelly says, slow and thoughtful like she’s working it out herself as she’s saying it. “I haven’t seen him myself, but the city whispers about him constantly. Lex keeps him close at hand.”

    “Whispers what?”

    “That he is the son of Zeus,” Brainy says, his thick brows drawn. “Lex declared him so a few months ago. Took him up to the Pnyx and performed some feat of strength to prove his heritage.”

    Kara scoffs. “Sounds like a fraud.”

    “That must be why Lex is keeping us here,” Lena says, her voice heavy with the knowledge. “It isn’t just about me, anymore. If he already has some poor sod pretending to be a demigod on his side, now that he knows you actually have powers he’s going to want to use you the same way.”

    “Great,” Kara grumbles. “Whatever plan you all had to stop him would probably come in handy right now.”

    “Has there been any change since I left?” Lena asks, seeming like she knows the answer. J’onn shakes his head.

    “None. If anything, he’s gotten worse. Less predictable. And we’ve lost half of our spies in the household to his paranoia. He banishes anyone he suspects. He seems to have a new target every day.”

    “So what’s your solution?” Kara asks. She glances around the garden for anyone who might be listening, suddenly feeling a little paranoid herself, and moves closer to the group.

    “One that has been long in motion. I have studied the annals of history,” Brainy says in a low, urgent tone. “Empires rise and fall, consistent as the tides. We need something new.”

    “Like what?”

    “Rule by the populace,” he says with a passion Kara wasn’t expecting. “No kings, no tyrants – every eligible citizen gets a vote on how the city-state is run. An assembly.”

    “We have allies in other cities willing to follow suit if Athens takes the first step,” Kelly adds.

    “How do you plan on enacting something like that?”

    “Fomenting insurrection against Lex is a part of it. Securing allies in his inner circle and beyond,” J’onn says. “But Lena being sent to Corinth halted our plans entirely. And when news came of her ship sinking…”

    Kara bites the inside of her cheek. The reminder of Lena’s shipwreck is yet another reminder of the life they lost. The life she aches to return to. But as much as she wants to simply take Lena and fly to somewhere safe, Lena was right in her reticence to do so. Lex’s plan has the potential to be catastrophic. Kara couldn’t truly hold that on her conscience and live in peace.

    “You’re all very dedicated to this,” Kara says, tucking Lena’s hand tighter into the crook of her elbow. It’s comforting just to be touching her after such a long night apart.

    “My city was annexed by Lex. One of the first casualties of his grand plan,” J’onn says, his deep voice cracking with emotion. “My family…didn’t make it. Lex and those like him need to pay for what they’ve done.”

    Brainy puts a consoling hand on J’onn’s shoulder. “My loss is less grievous, but I still miss my Nia every day. My love. My muse.” His eyes are soft and faraway. Kara nods sympathetically.

    “I’m sorry for your losses. I lost my family, too. A long time ago.”

    “My Nia, at least, is only in Sparta,” Brainy explains with a sigh. “She was banished for helping me with a play which voiced dissent to Lex’s rule.”

    “They’ve all lost things to Lex’s greed,” Lena says quietly. “It’s what brought us together.”

    “Are you truly back, Lena?” Kelly asks, her large, expressive eyes full of emotion. “We can’t do this without you. Will you help us?”

    Lena swallows. She doesn’t answer right away – instead, inexplicably, she looks to Kara with eyes like a stormcloud of inner conflict.

    “I’m with you,” Kara says quietly, but with a surety she hopes Lena feels. “To the end. Whatever you want to do.”

    Lena is spared from giving a definitive answer by the shutters above them opening loudly, and a vaguely familiar female servant leans out the window with a dusty woven rug and a stick to beat it with. She whistles as she works, an oddly specific tune, and it breaks the conversation like shattered glass.

    “We have lingered too long,” Brainy says, already breaking from the group. “We will meet again soon.”

    Kelly clasps Lena’s hand before she goes on her way as well, heading in the opposite direction with J’onn; she and Lena are left alone again but for the servant, who disappears as well a moment later. The shutters close loudly behind her. Lena leads Kara to a more secluded part of the garden, glancing furtively around them.

    “Is this what you meant when you said your freedom came at the expense of others?” Kara asks, stepping closer once they’re concealed behind a laurel shrub. “You felt guilty about abandoning your cause? Your friends?”

    Lena nods.

    “Do you hate me?” Lena’s voice quivers, her gaze turned to the ground.

     “What? Of course not. Why would you ever think that?”

    “I spent almost a year with you, letting my friends think I’d perished,” Lena says. She clears her throat of the quiver, straightening her shoulders. “I could have asked you to bring me back at any time, but I was selfish. They needed my help. The city needed my help, and I just…I just left them. To chase a dream.” Lena ends it with a bitter little laugh, and voyeurs be damned, Kara pulls Lena into a tight embrace. Lena melts into her, clutching at Kara’s shoulders under her chiton.

    “I could never hate you,” Kara says firmly, pressing a kiss to Lena’s hair. “And I could never hate any decision you made that let us have that dream, for a little while.”

    “It was a good dream, wasn’t it?” Lena’s voice is muffled in Kara’s shoulder, and Kara holds her tighter.

    “It was real,” Kara says fiercely. “It is real. And we’re going to get it back, even if I have to go through Hades.”

    Kara can feel Lena’s wistful smile against her collar. Lena pulls back from the embrace to cup Kara’s face, stroking the line of her jaw. Her expression is heartbreakingly sad. “I know you believe that. My Orpheus.”

    It’s a romantic thought, comparing Kara to the hero whose story brought about her revelation of love. Seeing that play had altered Kara’s life for the better. But there’s an undertone to the comment that leaves a pit in Kara’s stomach.

    Orpheus lost his love, in the end.

    Lena allows herself a single kiss, a quick press of lips before she pulls away to look around them for possible onlookers. Kara tries to savour the moment while she can.

    “We should be more careful,” Lena says. She puts some space between them, and every inch of it is painful.

    “I know. I hate this.”

    “It hurts me as much as it hurts you, love,” Lena whispers before she steps out into the open again, Kara a safe few paces behind. “Hopefully it’s not for much longer.”

     

     

    Dinner is yet another feast, and considering how little everyone else at the table actually eats of it Kara is beginning to think that wasteful overconsumption is the norm in this household. They pass over succulent steamed artichokes with garlic and dill and tender squid glazed with honey and garos; they merely pick at the soft leavened wheat-breads baked with fennel that feel like eating a warm cloud. They mostly drink wine and exchange vague, veiled conversation that makes Kara’s head hurt, and in an effort to keep quiet Kara ends up spending most of her time sampling the dishes in their stead.

    Lex’s friendliness wavers again when, near the end of the meal, Lena asks more firmly about what he wants from them.

    “You can’t avoid the question forever, Lex,” Lena says, her hand tight around her wine cup. “What will it take for us to leave?”

    “You know I can’t allow you to simply walk away,” Lex says in a quiet, dangerous voice now that the veiled pleasantries are over. “You’re still of my blood. You have a role to play. I can’t give your hand to any random peasant and have you go live in squalor on some farm, powers or no powers.”

    “But?” Lena says tightly.

    “But,” Lex repeats, his grin widening, “If Kara here revealed herself to the city as the daughter of Athena, and affirmed my rule? Perhaps I could be convinced to let you go. After a time. Who would I be to deny a demigod her wishes?”

    “As if you need some false god to claim your due,” Lillian interjects between sips of wine. “I’ve told you time and time again, these strangers are unnecessary -”

    “And I have told you not to question me!” Lex shouts, so suddenly that even the servants waiting near the door startle at his tirade. Spittle flies from his mouth, spraying the table, and Lillian quiets again.

    Lex puts the friendly mask back on with a smile somehow even more eerie than before.

    “With Athena’s chosen at my side, the child of the very founding goddess of this city, how could anyone not bow to the power of Athens?” he finishes, wiping his mouth delicately. Kara swallows her bite of bread. Her mouth feels unnaturally dry.

    “And what if we refuse?”

    Lex stares at her silently for long, uncomfortable moments. His wide smile never budges. It stretches on until, without warning, he stands up and claps his hands together.

    “I think we’ve eaten our fill, haven’t we? Let us walk.”

    He strides out of the room with the clear expectation of being followed. Lena rises, sighing heavily, and Kara follows, leaving Lillian behind as Lex leads them out the front door. A palanquin is waiting near the street, draped in sheer white curtains - four men are sitting nearby, and they spring up to lift the whole thing onto their shoulders when Lex has taken his seat. It’s abundantly clear that she and Lena are meant to follow on foot.

    “Look at my city,” Lex says, gesturing at the clean, orderly streets around them as they walk. “My soldiers, patrolling to keep the citizens safe. What would this city be, if not for me? It used to be squalid.”

    Lena is silent, but she shares a look with Kara that tells her all she needs to know about how much of this so-called impact is in Lex’s imagination.

    “I trained them myself,” he continues. “I paid to forge their armour, their weapons. I built them war machines.”

    “James trained them,” Lena interjects. “And the money came from the treasury. From taxes.”

    “All of this will fall to ruin if we are not prepared when Persia comes,” Lex spits, the corners of his mouth starting to foam. The path they’re taking is leading them closer to a large temple in the distance, the streets turning from residential homes to soot-stained workshops and forges at the edges of the agora. Kara can hear the clashing of metal against metal nearby, loud rhythmic shouts and sounds of physical exertion. The walk is long, Lex pointing out various buildings or statues he – or, more accurately, the taxes he collects – have funded, and the men carrying Lex set him down gently when the journey ends far from the Luthor villa.

    “My training grounds,” Lex says proudly when they finally arrive outside what looks like a repurposed gymnasium. Rather than naked oiled athletes practicing sport, however, it sounds instead to be full of men running military drills. Lex exits the litter, gesturing for them to follow him inside.

    “More join my ranks every day,” he says, leading them past barracks and store-rooms full of practice weapons. “From the sons of magistrates, to peasants looking for a better lot in life.”

    “To pull themselves out of the poverty that your laws enforce on their families,” Lena says sharply. Lex’s face twitches – there’s a throbbing vein near his forehead that looks about to burst, but shockingly he controls himself. He instead leads them down a hallway and out into an open courtyard.

    This is where the drill sounds originate from, Kara can tell at first glance – but a first glance is all she gets. As soon as her eyes focus on the men in the courtyard she gets a flash of discomfort, her vision blurring and going bright; she cries out, pressing a hand to her eyes.

    Lena is at her side in a moment, though she doesn’t dare touch Kara in front of Lex. “Kara! What is it?”

    “Sorry,” Kara says, blinking hard and rubbing her eyes, but the discomfort doesn’t go away until she diverts her gaze to the ground. It’s like half the suits of armour being worn by the soldiers in the courtyard are especially bright and reflective, and she can’t see through them. She can barely even look at them directly. “This armour – what’s it made of?”

    Lex confirms her theory. “A lead alloy, mixed with iron. I use it for my elite guard. They’re in charge of training.” There’s something entirely too smug in his voice.

    “Lead,” Kara mutters, closing her eyes. “Right.”

    Lex’s lack of surprise is especially disconcerting.

    “Is seeing your training ground supposed to change our minds?” Lena asks, an edge to her voice now. “Intimidate us?”

    “No,” Lex says, still irritatingly cheerful. “But I do have something else that might do the trick.”

    A set of stone stairs are their next destination, hidden behind a heavy door in the gymnasium’s interior. They lead down into a dark passage under the ground, and Lex gestures to a passing soldier to bring him a torch before descending into the narrow stairwell. The passage is short, at least, its stone walls streaked with condensation from the cool air, but at the end of it is a surprise even more shocking than the lead armour upstairs.

    A man is sitting in the room at the end of the hallway, barely visible in the torchlight.

    “Is this a dungeon?” Lena says as they approach the man, who looks only at Lex. Kara and Lena might as well be invisible. “Are you keeping someone prisoner here?”

    “Of course not,” Lex says jovially. “Simply one of many homes for him. He likes to be away from the chaos of the city. Isn’t that right?”

    The man doesn’t answer. He’s pale and still, with short black hair and a muscular build, and he’s obviously tall even when he’s seated. His face might have been handsome if his eyes weren’t so hollow. They’re a dull grey, red-rimmed and sickly and only accentuated by the blood-red colour of his tunic. Even so, there’s something familiar about his face as Kara gets close enough to see the details; the shape of his mouth, the alignment of his features. The perfect curl at the front of his dark hair.

    Just like her uncle Jor-El.

    The torchlight flashes over a glint of metal hanging around his neck. A silver pendant. One bearing a symbol as familiar to Kara as her own name, because it is.

    Kara’s supper, so recently enjoyed, threatens to come up again.

    No.

    “…Kal-El?” Kara whispers.

    Behind her, Lena lets out a tiny gasp. A flicker of recognition crosses the man’s face, too - he looks to Kara briefly, but when Lex snaps his fingers once his eyes turn stony again. Still, it’s enough to confirm Kara’s thoughts.

    Rao. Not here. Of all places, of all times, this can’t be where she finally finds her cousin again.

    “Kal!” Kara says again, her voice catching on his name as she runs forward to kneel in front of him, gently touching his shoulder. “How did you get here? What are you doing with him?”

    Kal-El doesn’t respond.

    “Ah, you do know each other!” Lex says, his voice loud and unwelcome in the cramped space. “I hoped you would. Your powers seem to be a match.”

    Kara stands, moving in front of Kal and shielding him from Lex. “What is he doing here? Why are you holding him?”

    “He came to Athens not long after Lena left, asking to help. To be the city’s hero,” Lex explains, derision dripping from every syllable as he looks at them both with disdain. “A man with all the power of the supposed gods, and all he wants to be is a glorified errand-boy. He was becoming a bit of a nuisance after a while, sticking his principled nose where it didn’t belong. Pestering me with his morals. Gaining the love of the citizenry independent of me. But not anymore. Now he’s loyal, isn’t he?”

    Lex directs the question at Kal, who still says nothing. He stares straight ahead, his eyes empty.

    “What did you do to him?” Kara demands. She can feel her own eyes starting to glow, her fury bursting through her tenuous control, but Lex only laughs.

    “So angry! You really must keep that under control,” he says, sounding amused by the whole situation. “Clark here doesn’t do well with sudden noises in his current state.”

    “His name is Kal-El,” Kara growls. Lex smirks.

    “Not here, it isn’t. Here he is Clark. The son of Zeus, and the bodyguard of the great Lex Luthor. Just as you will be, my dear daughter of Athena.”

    “You’re deranged,” Lena whispers. She has a hand pressed to her mouth, looking between Kal and Lex with growing horror; the only person in the world, perhaps, who might understand the gravity of this moment. While Alex and Eliza have always known Kara had a cousin who was sent to Earth at the same time that she was, Kara had been too ashamed to tell them the whole story. But Lena knows. She’s told Lena about Kal-El many times, confessed in the dark privacy of their bedroom her ever-present gnawing guilt at never finding him. The sense of duty her parents instilled in her that never had the chance to be fulfilled.

    Lex’s calm never wavers.

    “Am I? With two gods at my side, none will question my authority. And I have you to thank for bringing me my second, dear sister. You were of use after all.”

    Lena looks like she’s going to be sick.

    “Kal, please,” Kara whispers, shaking Kal’s shoulder desperately. He hardly moves, though his outline starts to blur as Kara fights back tears. “Listen to me. It’s Kara. Kara Zor-El. Your cousin.”

    “I have no cousin,” Kal intones. “My people are dead. I have no family but the Luthors.” Silver lines shimmer up his neck as he says it, crawling under his skin towards his forehead and shining through the thin membrane like writhing snakes, and Kara takes a startled step back.

    “What’s wrong with him?” Lena asks faintly.

    Lex shrugs noncommittally. “Oh, I’ve no idea. One day some peon is trying to give me a worthless silver stone that fell from the sky as a replacement for the grain tithe he owed, and the next Clark touches it and becomes wonderfully malleable. A real stroke of luck. One might even say it was meant to be.”

    Kara can’t see any stone on his person, nothing she can remove to break whatever horrid curse Lex has him under. There’s nothing she can do except stare into her cousin’s unfocused eyes, willing him to recognize her.

    “If only I had more,” Lex sighs wistfully. “Alas, you’ll need to be kept in line using other means. Namely,” Lex’s voice drops into a serious, more dangerous register, “if you try to take Lena away from Athens, I’ll tell Clark to drop her from the acropolis.”

    Kara is in front of Lena in an instant. “You seriously think I’m going to let you hurt her?”

    Lex doesn’t say a word. He simply snaps his fingers twice, and Kal stands – his head almost scrapes the damp ceiling. A towering presence. He doesn’t frighten Kara, exactly, but it does drive home the point Lex is trying to make – that Kara might not be the most powerful person in the room anymore.

    “What do you command?”

    Lex’s eyes linger on Lena. He smiles.

    “Nothing for now. But be on guard for my instructions.”

    “Kara,” Lena says quietly, pressing a hand to Kara’s lower back where Lex can’t see. “It’s alright.”

    It doesn’t feel alright. In fact this whole situation is feeling increasingly not alright, and Kara can’t even talk to Lena about it as they leave they gymnasium to follow Lex back to the Luthor house. Clark is with them now, walking at Lex’s side – it’s easier to refer to him by the name Lex has given him, to divorce him in her mind from the cousin she lost - and every word they say will be overheard. She doesn’t even dare sneak to Lena’s rooms again before bed. Clark is given the rooms next to Kara’s, and his powers match her own; if he doesn’t hear them talking from across the villa, he could easily see or hear Kara in the halls even at top speed.

    Instead she lies in the dark, staring up at the canopy and trying to come to terms with this on her own.

    Her cousin is here. Kal-El – Clark – is here, in some sort of trance and working for Lex, and her powers aren’t a guarantee of keeping Lena safe anymore. Any tiny speck of remaining hope she had of getting Lena out of the city soon is dead; and now, she has two people to worry about. She can’t just leave her cousin behind. Not without at least trying to bring him back to Rao’s light.

    And once her cousin is freed and Lena is safe, Lex Luthor is going to pay for what he’s done.

     

     

    Kara’s new assignment begins in the morning.

    A new tunic is brought to her, a light garment of deep Athenian blue with a long chlamys draped over the shoulders and a much longer hem than she’s used to. Her old sandals are swapped for new ones stitched with gold thread that strap all the way up her calves, and two twisting golden armbands with the Luthor signet sit atop the pile along with a matching brooch-pin for her cape. Kara puts it all on with a heavy heart, and the servants who brush her hair into shining blonde waves and lead her to the dining room won’t even look at her directly. 

    In fact from the moment she leaves her rooms everyone in the palace treats her strangely, besides Lena. They skirt around her where they didn’t before, avert their eyes out of a terrified sort of respect. Nobody speaks to her directly – they speak to Lena instead, like she’s some sort of vessel for Kara’s will.

    Well, there’s one other person in the compound who doesn’t treat Kara with reverence.

    “Why don’t you and your kin just fly yourselves back to the barbaric hole you crawled out of?” Lillian hisses at Kara in the mid-afternoon, accosting her in a narrow hall during one of the few moments that Kara is alone. Kara has been wandering the house, trying to memorize its layout and using her x-ray vision to map its hidey-holes, and she’s so focused on figuring out whether the obscured room on the other side of the wall is lined with lead that Lillian manages to sneak up on her without much trouble.

    “I’m not going anywhere without Lena,” Kara says once she’s recovered from the initial shock of ending up eye-to-eye (or as close as can be, when Lillian is several measures taller) with Lex’s mother.

    “Take her, if you want her so badly,” Lillian snarls. “She’s been nothing but trouble since the day her whore mother sent her here. More trouble than any marriage alliance is worth.”

    “Try convincing your idiot son of that,” Kara snaps. Lillian’s eye twitches. Her posture changes, stretching out to her full height and bearing down on Kara in a tirade.

    “Lex is the only hope the Greek world has of weathering the Persian storm,” Lillian says, the same mania Kara has seen in flashes from Lex taking full root in her voice. “And he certainly doesn’t need godless heathens like you two to help him.”

    Kara shakes her head. “You’re delusional. You both are.”

    “So help me, the moment I see even the slightest opportunity to be rid of you, I will -”

    “Mother!” Lex’s voice cuts through the rant, appearing at the end of the hall like a bald-headed spectre, and Kara takes a grateful step backward. Lex is the far worse option between the two, but anything that makes Lillian stop talking is welcome. Lena is at his side, which is both a relief and a cause for worry. “I hope you’re making our guest feel at home.”

    “Of course,” Lillian says, sickly-sweet and barely covering her contempt. Kara rolls her eyes.

    “I’d feel much more at home if Lena and I were allowed to leave the estate,” Kara says. Lex hardly bears her words a thought.

    “All in good time. Follow me, if you would?”

    Kara, understanding that this is less a request than a requirement, stomps after him with one last glare in Lillian’s direction.

    “Where are we going?” Kara asks. Lena is silent, following Lex with her eyes set on the floor as they make their way out of the house. Lex doesn’t even look at either of them as he answers. His eyes are all for Clark, who stands near the front doors in a full suit of bronze armour with a crested helmet. It’s too shiny to have ever been used in actual combat, styled to resemble Herakles with a dark lion’s pelt around his shoulders, and he looks every bit the part of a demigod. Just as Kara must as well, now, with her new clothes.

    “To your grand reveal, of course,” Lex says with a flourish, taking a long golden spear from one of his guards and handing it to her. It matches her arm-bands, as shiny as Clark’s cuirass. “Try not to speak too much. It ruins the illusion.”

    The request is much harder to follow when it comes from Lex rather than Lena. Rather than a palanquin today Lex has provided chariots, a large one for himself and two smaller ones for Clark and Kara, and Lena is instructed to stay behind and wait for them to return. Kara climbs into her chariot reluctantly, looking back to where Lena waits in the doorway – Lena’s face is a mask just as it’s been for almost every moment of their time here, but Kara knows her eyes well enough to see that she’d rather not be left here while Kara is marched off to be presented to the world. And every separation between them, no matter how short or long, always leaves Kara feeling off-kilter. Like the ground beneath her feet isn’t solid anymore.

    In this strange formation, Lex at the head and Kara and Clark following, they take to the streets.

    The effect is immediate. People had paid them little mind yesterday, simply moving out of the way for Lex’s procession without giving a second look – now, they stare. They gaze at Clark, and now Kara, with a reverence that makes Kara’s skin crawl. People practically jump out of the way to let them pass, aided by the herald Lex has apparently employed to shout Kara’s arrival to the heavens.

    “Make way!” the reedy little man shouts, his voice louder than his body should allow. “Make way for the tyrannos and his godly patrons! The daughter of Athena has joined her kin to lend her support to the Luthors!”

    It’s a long ride up to the Pnyx, one of the higher hills in the city, where Lex dismounts his chariot near a large open area paved with massive stones. A long, rectangular wooden platform almost resembling a stage has been built there and a crowd is gathering around it already, murmuring amongst themselves about the new demigod. Clark takes his place at Lex’s side, and with a sense of foreboding Kara follows.
