    “Citizens of Athens!” Lex says, stepping up onto the platform to call over the crowd with his hands spread wide. “My people. I come to you with the blessing of yet another of our Olympian gods. Athena has joined Zeus in endorsing my rightful rule, and her daughter stands beside me!”

    He looks to Kara, his eyes sharp, and Kara’s grip tightens around her spear.

    “She brings her mother’s sacred approval for the Luthor family. Kara, if you would demonstrate your divinity to the good people of this city?”

    Kara grits her teeth. Brainy had mentioned that Clark showed off his strength when Lex brought him here months ago, and for the sake of individuality Kara instead takes to the sky; up she flies, up and up as the crowd gasps and her cloak flutters dramatically in the high wind. It’s a brief moment of freedom, one where she can imagine leaving for good – finding Lena and flying her far away from here. But it doesn’t last.

    Lex claps loudly, and the crowd follows his example.

    “Wonderful!” Lex calls, waving her back down. “You have seen Zeus’ strength in Clark first-hand, and now Athena’s blessing is given. Walk the crowd, both of you – let them see you up close. Let them taste of the gods, thanks to their beloved tyrannos.”

    Kara lands lightly, straightening her cloak. The crowd advances as soon as her feet touch the ground.

    Some people only seem to want to touch her, grabbing at her arms or her clothes and then staring at their hands as if they can see the godly residue on their fingertips. Still others ask her for things. Favours from the gods, promises of good fortune or direct blessings. They ask her to speak to Asclepios to heal their relatives of sickness, to Aphrodite to fix their marriage troubles, to Demeter to bring them a good harvest; one sobbing woman even falls at Kara’s feet, grabbing at Kara’s legs to beg her to ask Hades to bring her child back to life. A soldier. Killed in one of Lex’s campaigns, no doubt.

    “Please,” the woman hiccups, tears streaming as she bows her head near Kara’s knees. “He is all I have. My Dorieus, my baby boy…please. You are divine, you could speak with Hades, your kin, you could ask – ”

    “Back, woman,” one of Lex’s guards interrupts loudly, hitting the woman with the butt of his spear until she lets go of Kara’s robes. “Loosen your grasp!”

    “Hey!” Kara snaps, pushing the guard away from the stooped woman almost hard enough to knock him over. He stumbles, caught and held up by two other guards. “Leave her alone!”

    Kara turns to the woman, now cowering, and helps her gently to her feet.

    “My apologies,” the woman sniffles, hunched over even while standing, tears streaking now through the dust on her face with her eyes cast down at Kara’s sandals. “I did not mean to offend the daughter of Athena. Please forgive me.”

    “You haven’t offended me. I swear,” Kara says, her heart fracturing in preparation to deliver a worse blow. “But I can’t…I can’t speak to Hades for you. It just doesn’t work that way.”

    The woman’s face crumples. She heaves a great sob that wracks her entire body, and Kara can’t help but flinch.

    “I’m so sorry,” Kara says brokenly, squeezing her hands. “Truly. I’m sorry, I wish I -”

    The woman falls into Kara’s arms, weeping into her cloak, and Kara’s heart aches for her even as she truly understands something for the first time. She’s reminded of Alex’s words, just before she met Lena. When she longed for more out of her life. When the idea of this sort of notoriety and ability to make a difference would have seemed exciting.

    They’ll think you’re a demigod, Kara. They’ll tear you apart to get close to a piece of divinity.

    Kara knows, now, as the grieving woman is carried away by a younger woman who looks like her daughter only to be replaced by more people. More grasping hands, more requests. It’s endless.

    Alex was right.

    Clark walks through the crowd like a shell of a man, unaffected by their pleas. Though in fact people seem less fixated on him than on Kara – nobody reaches out to touch him, and few people even speak in his direction. They direct their eyes elsewhere. Like they fear him. And Kara can see why. Clark’s eyes dart around like he’s looking for threats in every face. He seems to see through everyone, including Kara – Kara is certain that if she got that silver stone Lex mentioned away from him it would snap him out of it, but she has no idea where on him it’s kept, and she can’t risk him hurting anyone in the inevitable fight.

    On the ride back to the house, once the crowd has had their fill of her, Kara draws her chariot even with Lex’s on one of the wider streets.

    “How long am I going to have to do this?” Kara asks over the rumble of wooden wheels on stone. “You’ve announced my support, now. When does the charade end?”

    “You’ll do it for as long as I require it,” Lex says simply. Kara’s hand tightens on the reins, the leather cracking under the pressure, and she only barely manages not to let loose two beams of heat-vision right in his smug face.

    “You can’t keep us here forever,” Kara says sharply. “Even with my cousin at your side, eventually Lena will find a way around you. She’s smarter than you and you know it.”

    That gets a reaction from Lex. He turns to her, his face twitching.

    “My sister is a half-metic of base birth, barely literate only by the grace of begging for scraps from my tutors. She spent her childhood pleading for my attention and approval and when she didn’t earn it she tried to turn against me. She is nothing,” Lex snarls, tugging on his reins so hard in his anger that he brings the whole procession to a halt. “The only useful thing she ever did was provide me with another false god for my collection.”

    He ends his rant panting with fury, a fury he doesn’t cover up with his usual mask quite fast enough. It slipped further than usual. It’s more than he’s ever revealed to Kara; a glimpse at the weakness and insecurity that fuels his obsession.

    “You’re terrified of her,” Kara says quietly, following suit when Lex snaps the reins again. “It’s why you tried to have her sent away. And I can’t wait to see her best you.”

    Lex gathers himself, his face turning stony as he looks away from Kara and toward the streets ahead of them.

    “We shall see.”

     

     

    Kara wakes to suffocation.

    The first thing that strikes her in the confusion is that she can’t see. Everything is white, stark and painful even if she closes her eyes - it’s a blinding like she’s never felt. Even in the most moonless, starless night she can always see. Her x-ray vision has never left her completely sightless before now.

    Kara strains to hear something, anything to explain what’s happening, but that’s a dead end even more terrifying than her sight being stolen. There’s no sound. No birds or the movement of people in the city, no running water or buzzing bees. Not even Lena’s heartbeat to follow. Just a terrible, ringing silence. Her own breathing, harsh and panicked, and her hammering heartbeat. A silence worse than even the Phantom Zone. There she at least had the quiet mechanical whirr of her pod to stave off the endless vacuum of space. Her pulse is pounding, terror and remembrance making sweat form at her hairline and slide down her temple, and it’s that tiny thing – the glide of a single warm droplet from her forehead to the shell of her ear, pooling there and starting to cool – that grounds her.

    She can’t hear or see, but she can still feel. When she’d been young and prone to attacks like this when everything became too loud Eliza had always told her to listen to her other senses. The ones not hyper-tuned from solar radiation. Taste, touch, smell. Things she knows to be real and immediate. So Kara closes her eyes, and takes a breath through her nose.

    Metallic. The air is warm and stuffy but she can breathe, and heavy metal is the predominant smell. As for touch, she can feel the bed underneath her still – soft blankets and a downy mattress. She feels her way along the bedding to find a cool, metallic barrier on either side of her, and following it up by touch alone tells her that it rises above the bed in a curve.

    “What’s going on? What is this?” Kara calls out. She can hear her own voice, though it’s muffled and echoing.

    She’s in a dome.

    With a sudden desperate kick she makes contact with the top of the dome, sending it flying toward the ceiling and rolling out from under it with a burst of speed, and in a rush all sound and light returns to her at once. She’s still in her rooms, and it’s well past dawn. The dome has cracked the stone ceiling above her bed where it hit and fallen upside-down, rolling across the floor like a great spinning top, and Lex is standing nearby watching the whole thing with great amusement.

    “That, my friend, is pure lead,” he says cheerfully.

    Kara, still shaky from the panic of waking up to two senses cut off completely, stands up slowly.

    “Your cousin informed me of all your weaknesses,” Lex elaborates, his fingers steepled together. “I hoped he could get it on you while you slept – I do love being right. You didn’t hear a thing last night.”

    You didn’t hear a thing last night. Lex doesn’t even have to say Lena’s name for Kara to know – he’s done something to her, and isn’t making any effort to hide it. Lead or not, Kara failed to keep Lena safe. The panic returns with a vengeance. Kara takes a step backward in the direction of Lena’s end of the house, trying to focus her newly-returned hearing on Lena’s heartbeat. But it’s nowhere to be found.

    “Where is she?” Kara asks, wishing her voice was stronger than it is. She’s still trying to seek Lena out, filtering past layers of audio input, but she can’t find a trace over the chaos of Athens.

    “She’s hidden. Perfectly safe, I assure you,” Lex says, snapping his fingers in that horrible, expectant way he has. “At least, she will be so long as you do as you’re told.”

    Several guards enter carrying something that makes Kara’s eyes hurt again, though not quite so badly as the dome – a suit of armour, much like Clark’s. It’s tailored to her frame and inlaid with motifs of Athena interspersed with the Luthor crest. Breastplate, pteryges, greaves, and a large decorative helmet, with a spear and shield to complete the illusion. All appearing to be crafted of shining gold with blue linen accents, but it’s only a veneer covering what’s underneath. Pure lead. For all intents and purposes, a muzzle for Kara’s senses.

    “And I’m just supposed to trust your word?” Kara says, already making a checklist in her mind of all the places Lena might be kept and how she can get there as fast as possible. “I’ll find her. And then I’ll find you.”

    “Mmm. Right. But will you find her before I give the order for her to be killed, or after?”

    That casual threat, even the idea of Lena coming to harm, is more than Kara can take. She flies forward to grab Lex by the front of his robe and slam him into the wall, her eyes glowing with a fire that only seems to make Lex’s grin wider.

    “Lead only dampens my hearing and sight,” Kara growls. “I could kill you where you stand and not lose an hour of sleep over it.”

    And in this moment it truly feels, in Kara’s heart of hearts, like those words are true. She’s never taken a life, nor has she ever wanted to. She’s never considered herself a person prone to violence. But to protect Lena? There’s nothing on this earth she wouldn’t do.

    “And Lena dies with me,” Lex says matter-of-factly. He doesn’t seem to fear for his life at all, and it’s all the more infuriating. “And I wouldn’t lose sleep, either. If you so much as stray from this property without my permission, Kara. Put a single toe out of line. If my men see a momentary blur in the sky that could be you? Lena dies.”

    Kara’s stomach lurches.

    “I’d catch a messenger before they could deliver the order,” Kara says. Her grip on him doesn’t loosen.

    “Ah, but could you catch your own kin?” Lex gestures at a new figure in the doorway – Clark steps from the shadows, emotionless, his eyes glowing to match Kara’s. “A man whose powers match your own? Are you willing to wager that you’d get there before he could snap her pretty little neck?”

    With a pained roar, Kara presses him harder into the wall. “She’s your sister. Are you truly such a monster?”

    He wheezes, Kara’s fist squeezing the air from him, but he puts up a hand to stop Clark when he takes a defensive step forward.

    “How much blood are you willing to have on your hands?” Lex whispers with the last of his breath.

    “He’s weakened by whatever you’ve done to him,” Kara says, glancing at Clark’s pallor. His red-rimmed eyes, and the veins visible through his pale skin. “Your silver stone.”

    “I’ve been calling it Kryptonite,” Lex rattles, cheerful even with Kara crushing his lungs. “After your dear home planet. So fascinating.”

    Kryptonite.

    Lex knows about Krypton. He knows so much more than she hoped – has knowledge that she herself doesn’t have, in fact, with his knowledge of how this silver Kryptonite works, and this time Kara can guess at where it came from.

    “He’s sick. I’d beat him there. I could protect her.” But her grips slackens slightly, just enough to let him rasp a few breaths.

    “Are you absolutely sure about that?” Lex says, feigning thoughtfulness. He seems to make himself comfortable even with his feet several inches off the ground and Kara’s hands compressing his chest, not a hint of fear in his face despite how close he came to crushed ribs. He looks absolutely delighted by the entire situation. “How blasé you are with your lover’s life. One might think you don’t truly love her at all.”

    Kara hasn’t been playing this political game nearly long enough to keep the shock off her face. They weren’t careful enough. Lex knows exactly to what degree Lena is the crux of her priorities, and he’s done exactly what Lena feared – he’s used it against them. Lex found Clark’s vulnerability before Kara even arrived, and now he’s found Kara’s.

    She can’t let anything happen to Lena.

    Kara drops him to the floor.

    “You know nothing about how I feel,” Kara says shakily. Lex chuckles, dusting off his robe as Clark helps him to stand up again.

    “Lena might be deft enough to fool me but you, my Kryptonian friend, wear your heart on your face.”

    “How much has Kal told you about our kind?” Kara asks. Lex tilts his head in an expression so patronising that Kara has to clench her fists to keep from punching it off his face.

    “Enough. Now, what do you say? Do as I command, and let Lena live…or kill me, and lose her too?”

    There’s no decision, in the end. Only one choice is acceptable. Kara puts the armour on, cutting off her senses piece by piece, and with a silent vow to find a way to make this right at any cost she submits to Lex’s will.

     

                                                                                                                  

    The following days are torturous. Lex’s plans are clear, now that this stage is in motion and he doesn’t need to dance around revealing them – his intent is to invade the other city-states with Kara and Clark at the head of his armies. His secret weapons.

    At his demand she dons her lead armour every morning and, blinded and deafened, performs godhood for the masses. He parades her around like a prized horse, bidding her to bless his allies in ostentatious ceremonies at the temple and lend Athena’s support to his rulership. All while dangling Lena’s safety in front of her. Clark hardly joins them; while Kara is busy he takes his rest, and by night he watches her sleep, ensuring she doesn’t leave the premises to seek Lena out. No matter how many pleas Kara makes to the cousin she knows is somewhere within him, he’s deaf to it. Bent to Lex’s will, and completely willing to hurt Lena if Kara pushes too hard.

    Even the luxuries of living in the Luthor estate offer barely a moment’s distraction. The rich foods she’s plied with at every meal lose their taste; the daily hot baths Lex insists on to maintain her image of godliness are nothing but a reminder of the wonderful day she spent with Lena in Megara what feels like a lifetime ago. Servants are always available to get her whatever she requests, but she hates calling on them. It all makes for a beautiful and intentionally comfortable cage.

    The days drag on in this terrible, endless cycle until Kara is at the end of two full weeks of this excruciating circus. Two weeks without even a confirmation that Lena is actually unharmed, and Lex hasn’t already hurt her without Kara even knowing. All she has to go on is his word. Lena’s friends are no help, avoiding Kara entirely to keep Lex from suspecting them of treason, and as much as Kara knows its necessary all she wants to do is gather them all up and shake them. To ask them all the questions that burn in her mind every night as she lays awake, surrounded by lead and watched by Clark, wondering where Lena could be. What’s going on? How have you not found her yet?

    Are you even trying?

    Lex throws a symposium at the end of the second week.

    Kara is of course expected to attend and entertain the guests, as Lex makes very clear at breakfast the morning of the party. She isn’t expected to be in full regalia this time, at least – she’s back to the blue robe and arm-bands, only this time with an ornate golden lead-lined headpiece that sits heavy across her brow like a hybrid of a helmet and a crown. It’s incredibly ostentatious, but the whole thing paired with Kara doing her best to be somewhat personable under Lex’s watchful supervision seems to dazzle almost everyone at the party.

    Besides J’onn and Brainy, who are in attendance but skilfully avoid making eye contact, there’s only one person who doesn’t fawn from the moment Kara enters the hall. She’s sleek and dark-haired, beautiful in a dangerous, almost predatory way, and when she isn’t hanging off Lex’s every word or competing for his attention with one of the hetaerae Kara often sees him with these days she’s staring at Kara with an intensity that makes Kara’s skin crawl.

    Kara does her best to avoid the woman. It’s easy at first – everyone Lex has invited, from city officials to artists, wants to talk to Kara for the first few hours. They ask endless questions as the party drags on; as more wine is consumed the questions turn from inquiries about her godly heritage to drunken requests for party-tricks using her powers, and finally as darkness falls outside and the air in the room gets overly warm and rambunctious they take a hard turn into the inappropriate.

    “Oh, good show,” one heavily inebriated playwright claps as Kara holds the heavy marble couch above her head, both the ladies currently sitting on it giggling loudly over being lifted so easily. “Truly, I’ve never seen anything like it from one with your physique!”

    Kara can only assume that he means the physique of a woman, considering the weedy man’s muscle definition is less than her own. He certainly seems interested in said physique, reaching out to touch her bicep without asking, and it takes a great deal of self-control not to shake his hand off right away. She fakes a small laugh.

    “Well, that’s Athena’s bloodline for you. Right?” Kara says, hoping her smile doesn’t look like a grimace as she lowers the chair back to the ground.

    “Such a lovely physique it is,” drawls a woman Kara recognizes as the head priestess from the temple of Aphrodite. Her voice is low and musical to match the silky sheen of her skin, and Kara can understand why she excels in the position she holds. “I must ask, Kara. Do you share your Olympian family’s penchant for more carnal activities?”

    “What?” Kara squeaks, dropping the chair the final few inches. It makes a loud noise when it hits the stone floor, the woman seated on it squealing, and the ruckus makes half the party look their way.

    “You know, I’ve been wondering that all night,” another nearby hetaerae pitches in. Kara has never met this one before tonight, so she isn’t one of Lex’s usuals – she stands out in the crowd, tall and willowy and stunningly beautiful in a way that doesn’t move Kara at all. It’s difficult to find beauty in anything lately when Lena consumes her every thought. Her smile is lovely at least, if a bit overly friendly.  “Have you ever lain with a mortal?”

    “Uhh,” Kara stammers, taking a few steps backward and out of her reach. “That’s sort of…private?”

    “I think that’s a yes!” the hetaerae giggles, finishing the wine in her cup and handing it to a passing servant for a refill. She flicks her long hair over her shoulder, leaning close but not yet breaking the barrier of touch. “That strength…I can imagine it has a few practical applications.”

    “Don’t get too excited, Samantha,” the priestess chuckles while Kara tries not to outwardly panic. “Remember the tales of Zeus’ mortal lovers? You don’t want to end up turned into a swan.”

    “I think I might not mind, if a night with Kara here preceded it.”

    The whole party laughs, and Kara – with a little help from a burst of superspeed – darts away from the group while they’re distracted by their own terrible jokes. She skirts around the edges of the room, passing J’onn and Brainy with a curt nod, until she’s reached the banqueting table and grabbed her fifth plate, and it’s here that the Lex’s dark-haired companion finally approaches. Kara feels her sidle up while she’s stacking the plate high with stress-snacks, and fights the urge to roll her eyes. She’s beyond tired of these social games.

    “Mercy Graves,” The woman says as an introduction, extending her hand daintily to Kara. “Lex is a dear friend of mine.”

    Kara nods tightly, shifting her plate to the opposite hand and shaking Mercy’s arm firmly. Mercy seems surprised by that – she’d likely been expecting to have her knuckles kissed, but Kara is far from in the mood for that kind of sycophantic display.

    “Kara,” she says gruffly. Marcy takes her arm back, looking Kara up and down.

    “I must commend him on finding you. A much better representation of the gods than that dreadful moody thing he had before. Clark, was it?”

    “Mmm,” Kara says, shoving her mouth full of stuffed grape leaves. Mercy continues regardless of Kara’s disinterest.

    “Impossible to talk to, that one. No personality whatsoever,” Mercy drawls, swirling her wine. “You, though – you’re quite a creature, aren’t you? Lex really outdid himself.” She’s regarding Kara like a specimen, something to be examined and dissected, and Kara grits her teeth.

    “Lex didn’t find me. Lena did,” Kara says shortly, swallowing her mouthful of food. Mercy hums.

    “Ah, dear Lena. She’s always been like a sister to me. A pity she couldn’t be here,” Mercy says with no real pity in her voice at all, her sharp eyes trained on Kara’s face. Studying her for a reaction. “She has more pressing duties to attend to. Rather tied up at the moment. Or, so I hear.”

    There’s something just a little bit too smug about Mercy’s tone. Something intentionally meaningful to her so I hear. It reminds Kara of Lex’s stupid, arrogant face every time he reminds Kara of why she’s doing this; it makes Kara want to throw Mercy into the nearest wall and demand she reveal what she knows. The plate shatters in Kara’s hand, sending shards of clay and bits of food all over the stone floor, but before Kara can commit to doing anything rash Mercy is already leaving, giggling into her cup. Across the room Lex breaks away from the conversation he was having, and Mercy drifts away to take advantage of his freed attention.

    “Lovely to meet you,” Mercy says over her shoulder, laughing again when Kara crushes the remaining clay shards into dust in her hand.

    A new plate heaped with food appears at Kara’s elbow, held by the hetaerae Kara only just escaped from. A better alternative to Mercy, but still not ideal company.

    “Thanks,” Kara grumbles, taking the plate but finding her appetite is lost. “Samantha, right?”

    “Sam. Pretend I’m still flirting with you,” Sam says, stepping close and fussing coquettishly with Kara’s cape. Her face tells the truth, though – she doesn’t look flirty in the least. And her voice is solemn.

    “…okay?” Kara says. Sam drapes her arms around Kara’s shoulders.

    “Ignore Mercy,” Sam murmurs. “She’s pathetically in love with Lex, and when he gives his attention to her girls instead she feels the need to take her pettiness out on others.”

    The drunkenness from before is nowhere to be found in Sam’s words. Instead of propositions she’s speaking sense, and though Kara has no idea what to do with her hands – one is still holding a plate, and it feels too inappropriate to put the other anywhere else – she doesn’t step away.

    “Her girls?”

    “Us. Mercy Graves is the facilitator of all things illicit in this city,” Sam says under her breath, punctuating it with a fake giggle that diverts attention away from them. “Proprietress of every brothel, and queen of all hetaerae who want to ply their trade here. She takes a cut of all our profits in exchange for networking for clients. And she has Lex’s complete confidence.”

    Kara blinks. Sam’s demeanour has done a complete flip between their two conversations, and she’s still not sure which of the two personalities to trust. “Why are you telling me this?”

    “Because she is a very dangerous enemy to make,” Sam says, her eyes boring into Kara’s as she slowly enunciates every word. “Do you understand?”

    If there’s a hidden meaning behind Sam’s words, Kara can’t parse it.

    “Why can’t anyone here speak in plain words?” Kara grumbles.

    She unfortunately says it a bit too loudly, and Lex and Mercy look their way even from across the room. Sam’s back is to them, but Kara can see that she senses their curiosity – she laughs, loud and artificial, and leans in close to speak directly in Kara’s ear. “You know why. Lena warned you before you came here.”

    Kara’s breath catches. She seizes Sam’s sleeve, heart pounding, grasping the silky material. “How do you know that? Have you – have you talked to her? Where is she? Do you -”

    She’s succinctly silenced by Sam’s lips, pressed to hers in a sudden and forceful kiss.

    It’s over before Kara can truly process it’s happened, but it’s more than enough to disrupt Kara’s questions and leave her speechless and blinking in Sam’s face. Her lips are slack, but Sam’s are whispering something.

    “Have faith. And play along.”

    Sam pushes Kara away playfully, and her next words are said with a drunken lilt and loudly enough to be heard by the entire room. “The first one was free, but you know my rates, daughter of Athena. Even for a god I charge what I’m worth. You know where to find me if you decide to cough up the coin.” She ends with a wink, and saunters back to her priestess friend while Kara stares at her retreating form, mouth agape.

    She’s left with even more questions than she had before, and very little inclination to heed Sam’s words. It’s clearer than ever that nobody here can be trusted to be who they seem – Sam could be an ally, or she could be simply a ploy from Lex and Mercy to keep Kara in line. A clever ruse. Another attempt to dangle Lena in front of her.

    Overwhelmed by the noise of the party even with her hearing dulled, Kara storms out of the room and towards the gardens. She can’t be in this stuffy, horrid house a moment longer; she needs fresh air and sky.

    She sits heavily on the first stone bench she finds, burying her face in her hands, and fights what feels like a losing battle not to cry. It’s all too heavy to carry, suddenly. There’s no easy solution, no cut-and-dry way to get out of this endless hell, and she has no way of knowing what Lena would want her to do. Would she approve of Kara going along with Lex’s manipulation, like Sam said? Is she conscious of the situation? Or is she unaware and in pain somewhere far away, needing Kara’s help? Wondering why Kara hasn’t found her yet?

    The last thought is too painful to think about.

    With her hearing muffled by the stupid headpiece Kara doesn’t hear Brainy following her into the garden until he’s seated next to her.

    “Clark is resting, and Lex is drunk and distracted,” Brainy says quietly, glancing around them for eavesdroppers. “We can speak for a moment.”

    Kara heaves a great, unsteady sigh. “I can’t do this, Brainy. I’m not a politician. I can’t just wait around. I need to look for her. I need you to be looking for her.”

    “We must be patient,” Brainy counters, making a hushing motion with his hand when Kara speaks too loudly. “We have moles in the closest part of Lex’s ranks, and forcing them to reveal themselves could reveal us before the time is right. You are becoming reckless.”

    “I don’t care about that!” Kara says, loudly. Recklessly, even. “I care about Lena not being imprisoned!”

    “Lena is exactly where she needs to be. And she is being kept comfortably.”

    Being kept comfortably? There’s no way for Brainy to know that for sure, unless -

    “Do you know where she is?” Kara demands. She sits up straight, putting all the authority she can muster into the question, and Brainy shrinks from her a bit. From the command in her voice.

    “I know very little -”

    “But you have an idea! Does everyone know but me?”

    “I can assure you that is not true. I don’t know her location. I only know that she is not in any immediate danger,” Brainy says, pushing back just enough to make it clear he isn’t budging, no matter how much she tries to intimidate him. “However she will be if we move too rashly.”

    “And why can’t I know?”

    “None of us know, Kara!” Brainy says, almost an outburst before he catches himself and lowers his voice again. “We all care about her safety. Saying this out loud here is dangerous enough. You are practically screaming it to all of Athens.”

    “It doesn’t seem like you care about her safety. You’re putting her in danger for the sake of your political plans,” Kara says, standing from the bench to pace furiously in front of him. She knows he’s right, but that doesn’t make it any easier to swallow. “You’re supposed to be her friends!”

    Brainy shakes his head. “The plans are not mine. She does this on her own terms. I am sorry more can’t be shared with you, but I know almost nothing, for her well-being. Lena is the one at the helm. We are all simply playing our parts.”

    “How am I supposed to trust that?” Kara scoffs. Her blind pacing leads her right into a laurel bush – the same one Lena had kissed her behind weeks ago. Had that been the thing that sealed her fate? Had someone seen them? It’s impossible to tell, and the not-knowing has Kara wanting to rip the thing out of the ground. She has so much anger, and nowhere to put it. “I barely know any of you. You could be using her just like Lex is.”

    “I am sorry I cannot give you more reassurance,” Brainy says shortly, gathering his long robes and standing up. “For now, this is all I can do.”

    With a quick incline of his head he disappears into the house again, and Kara is left alone to fume in peace.

    The recklessness she was so warned against only gets worse in the following days. It’s a beast living inside her, one that rages every time she’s reminded of Lena’s absence; she starts talking back to Lex, bucking his authority just enough to make him angry. She corners J’onn in the agora in the middle of a public appearance, shrugging off her hangers-on to ask him in veiled words if they can meet later to discuss the situation, and Clark’s vaguely threatening presence is the only thing that keeps her from refusing to let him leave until he agrees. She stalks the halls of the Luthor house looking for any clues to Lena’s whereabouts, ignoring Lillian completely even as she peppers Kara with insults.

    In short, Kara does everything she can do short of simply leaving, kept within that line only by the threat to Lena’s safety, and it’s hard not to let the injustice of it all consume her.

    She’s on one of these fruitless clue-finding missions – in the servants’ quarters, this time, hoping to overhear a conversation or speak to someone in the few remaining daylight minutes she has before Clark usually wakes – when one such servant bumps into her so hard that the poor woman is almost sent sprawling by the force of Kara’s shoulder.

    “Fuck -” the woman hisses after she hits the wall, rubbing her shoulder but already hurrying away down the hall towards the kitchen. She’s vaguely familiar, but Lex has so many people in his household that it’s hard to keep track. “Goddamn battering ram.”

    A piece of papyrus flutters to the floor near Kara’s feet.

    “Sorry!” Kara says, wincing as she stoops to pick the papyrus up. “Hey, wait - you dropped something?” But by the time she looks up, the woman has disappeared into one of a dozen doors in this hallway without a trace.

    Kara frowns. The papyrus is small, and had it been folded Kara might have put it back where it fell and left it for the servant to reclaim privately. But it isn’t folded. It’s open, facing up so that the black ink on it is visible, and Kara’s heart leaps when she realizes what she’s holding.

    The papyrus bears only two words. Kryptonian characters, in Lena’s familiar hand, so small and innocuous that it could be mistaken for an idle drawing by anyone unfamiliar with the alphabet.

    Patience, Orpheus.

    The rush of pure, relieved joy Kara feels at seeing Lena’s handwriting can’t be stifled. It goes to her head like the small sips strong wine she’d been allowed on Krypton as a child; she’s dizzy with it, clutching the paper like a lifeline and bringing it to her lips like she can somehow extract Lena’s essence from the fibers. But the feeling is tempered, after a moment, by the meaning it holds.

    First and foremost, it’s confirmation that Lena is okay. Like Brainy said she isn’t in any immediate danger, at least not any that prohibit her from sneaking out one note. That element of Kara’s worry can be lessened. And with that assuaged, Kara can intuit what Lena is trying to tell her. Exactly what Brainy and Sam failed to.

    Lena has a plan get them out of this. There’s something happening behind the scenes, something Lena is working on, and Kara remembers Lena’s words from just before they arrived in the city. That she has to be different here. Lena wears a mask in Athens. Lena is smart, so much smarter than Lex believes her to be. And Kara needs to trust her.

    Brainy was right, as much as it pains Kara to admit it. The haze of terror and guilt Kara has been living in clears slightly, the warmth this one tiny note brings chasing it away, and the truth of the situation is left behind. If this all isn’t done properly it could all be for nothing. She could buck Lex’s authority and try to find Lena now, or try to insinuate herself fully into whatever Lena is planning, only to lose her in the end if Lex catches on. Every attempt either of them makes to communicate with each other is a risk that Lena will be hurt. One note might be all Kara will get. She has to play along with the game for Lena’s sake. To trust that Lena will be there when the time comes. She can’t look backward.

    She has to be better than Orpheus.

    Chapter End Notes

    Oh Eurydice, we're really in it now

    Terms I thought might be unfamiliar:

    Tyrannos – tyrant
    Chlamys – a short cloak usually worn by men
    Adelphé – sister
    Garos - Greek fermented fish sauce
    Metic – non-citizen, usually someone from another city-state. Most city-states had their own separate governments
    Pnyx – large open area on the big hill near the acropolis in Athens, where people often made speeches. Later it became where democratic debates happened
    Pteryges – armoured skirt worn by soldiers
    Symposium - a drinking party, usually thrown by rich citizens with hired entertainers
    Hetaerae – female entertainer. Similar to courtesans, but their roles were more social and they usually entertained at parties

    Chapter 4

    Chapter Notes

    There is some light violence (1-on-1 fight, a bit of blood) that I don’t think qualifies for the ‘graphic violence’ warning in this chapter, but if I’m wrong and you think I should change it please let me know

    HAPPY PRIDE

    Patience, Orpheus.

    Those two words from Lena stir up a brand-new determination in Kara.

    She commits herself fully to her part in this play, following Lex’s instructions and acting as his left hand without complaint. She keeps an eye out for the servant who dropped her the note, but only ever sees her in passing; it’s the same one Kara noticed the night she and Lena arrived, who looked so relieved to see Lena alive. Kara wants to ask her a hundred questions but they’re never alone, and she knows now not to make herself conspicuous. She doesn’t even know the woman’s name.

    If Lex is surprised by the sudden change he doesn’t say so. He only asks more and more of Kara by the day. More processions, more blessings, more public appearances. Clark joins them rarely, if at all. Lex claims that he needs rest, but as the days turn into weeks Kara develops another theory.

    Clark is volatile. He may be under Lex’s sway, sworn to protect him, but Lex doesn’t control his every impulse and those impulses are often violent and unpredictable – the people fear Clark, and fear Lex when he’s nearby. They give him a wide berth where they crowd Kara at every opportunity, and never is it more obvious than when Lex has them both present to open a new public building in the agora.

    After Lex’s speech he walks the crowd, shaking hands and making nice. When a commoner grabs at Lex’s robe he pays them little mind, but Clark doesn’t share his dismissal – he grabs the man by the throat, ripping him away from Lex with no warning whatsoever.

    “You do not touch the tyrannos,” Clark growls, lifting the man into the air until his face starts to purple. The poor man’s wife is screaming, pulling on Clark’s arm to no avail and begging him for mercy, and Lex is too busy chatting up a gaggle of sycophants to notice the commotion.

    Clark squeezes once, fingers digging in hard enough to kill, and Kara snaps.

    Her arm is around Clark’s neck in an instant in what would be a sleeping hold on anyone else. Clark is unaffected by the pressure, but it surprises him enough that he drops the man to focus on escaping. He twists in her grip, grunting when Kara manages to keep him in place, and finally resorts to slamming an elbow into her gut so hard that it bends her double. It’s a pain like she’s never known – she should have expected it, Clark being the only person on this planet whose strength matches hers, but it’s difficult to prepare for something she’s never experienced before – and she lets go of him to wheeze for air, only just managing to duck away from his next backhanded hit.

    “Cousin!” Kara shouts hoarsely in Kryptonian, shielding the crowd behind her with her own body. “Peace!”

    Either Clark doesn’t understand her, or he doesn’t care. He swings at her again, and with a surge of effort Kara catches his fist in her hand.

    A ripple goes through the crowd. Gasps and whispers, all shock and realization; they’ve likely never seen Clark disobeyed before, let alone stopped. His strength has never been questioned. Clark struggles against her grip, grunting as he tries to force his fist toward her face, but Kara holds him fast and with one quick movement, twists his arm down and hits him hard with her free hand.

    Her fist lands squarely on his jaw. The force of it sends him sprawling face-first into the dust. When he rises again, his eyes glowing, his skin is split where she struck him; a single drop of crimson blood is smeared against his cheek.

    Nearby, Kara can hear someone gasp.

    “He bleeds.”

    Clark’s focus has narrowed to Kara alone. Just as she intended. He ignores the people around them as he strides forward, his approach radiating pure aggression, his fist cocked, and Kara prepares to dodge the hit and deliver her own -

    “Enough!”

    The scuffle is apparently enough to finally alert Lex. His scream silences the crowd. Clark stops in his tracks like an obedient dog, the fire in his eyes dying down, and Kara manages to stop herself just before she hammers him in the jaw again.

    “Clark!” Lex snaps. “Back to the house.”

    For a single tense moment, Kara thinks that Clark might say no. His ire is directed at Lex for the first time in Kara’s memory, just on the edge of defiance. But in the end he flies silently into the air, disappearing over the city, and Lex turns instead to Kara.

    “It’s time we moved on.”

    He stalks back to the chariots, shouldering through the crowd which doesn’t part for him the way they usually might. The man Clark almost murdered is being helped into sitting by a few lookers-on, rubbing at his neck and taking great heaving breaths, and as Kara starts to follow Lex the man’s wife grabs Kara’s arm.

    “Thank you. You saved him,” the woman says, her eyes shining through streaked tears. “Thank you.”

    “The tyrannos’ attack dog goes too far,” says the man rubbing the victim’s back, his face set in grim anger. Another person speaks up from the crowd.

    “He is no demigod. He is a monster. He belongs in Tartarus.”

    “You don’t follow his orders, do you?” a third voice asks. Female this time, and youthful. “You’re different.”

    Kara bites at the inside of her cheek. She knows how she wants to respond, but -

    “Kara!” Lex barks, already in his chariot at the other side of the agora. “Come!”

    Kara looks to the mass of people before her. They’re all staring at her expectantly, faces full of what she suspects is hope. She does need to heed Lex to keep Lena safe, but while he’s occupied barking orders at his guards she lowers her voice, recalling Sam’s words.

    “Have faith,” she says quietly, giving them an encouraging smile. “I’m here to help. I swear it.”

    She flies to her chariot before Lex gets too impatient, landing lightly and waving back at the crowd as their procession starts to move. A great many of them wave back, and their encouragement sparks a new curiosity in her.

    Perhaps the people of Athens are less loyal to Lex than she thought. After such an eye-opening experience, the social climate of the city seems changed in her eyes.

    Perhaps it had been the shiny newness of Kara’s arrival, but she had been under the impression that the populace was generally happy with Lex’s rule. But she sees the cracks in the veneer now – people are frustrated and restless. They’ve all lost things to Lex’s warmongering, and continue to struggle under the austerity caused by his usage of their taxes to fund it. Clark has them all living in terror, any small attempt to rebel subsequently punished, and rather than stamping out the unrest Lex’s harsh policies have only made the resistance flourish. People curse him under their breath as he passes them in his endless processions, and Kara has the feeling that it’s only a fear of Clark and the peace of her own presence that keeps them from screaming their curses along with rotten vegetables.

    Kara is Lex’s shield right now. He needs her.

    With her focus on finding Lena somewhat calmed, she also takes more notice of what’s happening around her in Lex’s house. He often leaves his meetings frustrated, and though he conducts most of them in his study – the walls of which are, as she suspected, lined with a thin veneer of lead, keeping Kara out of the majority of his business – she occasionally overhears passing snippets when she manages to find some privacy to take her cursed helmet off.

    Lex is frustrated to the point of explosive fits of temper. Not only is the city on the verge of active revolt, but his grand plans aren’t going smoothly – twice in one week Kara hears reports that neighboring city-states he considered conquered already aren’t accepting his demands, and it only accelerates with time. Schemes coming to light, defecting allies, troop and supply deliveries gone awry; by just over a month since Lena disappeared, Lex is already losing control. The city’s garrison of soldiers gradually dwindles, unit after unit being sent off to quell unrest elsewhere, until Athens is left with barely a skeleton crew of militia.

    It would almost be entertaining to watch Lex slowly lose his grip if the stakes weren’t so high. Lex is hardly ever seen without an entourage of sycophants these days, even when Kara isn’t accompanying him. Most commonly it’s Otis Graves, whom Kara still harbours the deepest kind of resentment towards for his role in reporting Lena’s whereabouts to Lex all those months ago.

    The man is an idiot, but a talkative one. If nothing else he’s useful in letting other information slip outside the confines of Lex’s office – all Kara has to do is find a nook to hide in when Clark isn’t around, and she can often overhear entire conversations.

    “Sir, Corinth has rescinded their alliance,” Kara hears Otis say breathlessly a few weeks after receiving Lena’s note, as if he’s sprinted across town to give Lex the news. He seems to have found Lex as he walks between the estate and his now mostly-empty training grounds - Kara has ducked into a servant’s passage, taking the helmet and some of the armour off to listen for just such a tidbit, and she strains her ears to hear Lex’s growled reply a few streets over.

    “What?”

    “Morgan Edge claims that since he was not delivered the wife he was promised, the agreement is void. He won’t send his troops.”

    Kara hears something shatter, and a quiet protest quickly silenced – a merchant, maybe, whose wares Lex has destroyed in his anger.

    “Couldn’t we just send her to him, as we agreed?” Otis asks, entirely gormless. “What else is she good for, really?”

    “No, Otis. We couldn’t,” Lex says slowly, as if he’s talking to a disobedient dog. “If I send her away from Athens, she’s out of my control. And if she’s out of my control, so is the Kryptonian. Do you understand? Does that make sense in the empty, echoing chamber that is your idiot skull?”

    Otis swallows audibly.

    “Um. Yes?”

    So. Lena is still in the city. It gives Kara a rush of dizzying hope, knowing she hasn’t been taken too far, but Lex seems to be experiencing the opposite. He smashes something else with a frustrated yell, seeming to throw it at Otis if the larger man’s hiss of pain is any indication.

    “Someone did this,” Lex mutters, and Kara can hear his footsteps pick up again with Otis following. “He’s been turned against me. And we need to find out who.”

    Lex storms away with Otis trailing him, and Kara slips the armour back on and emerges from her hidey-hole to set the helmet back on her head before Lillian wanders by to berate her as she’s so fond of doing.

    Kara can see Lena’s fingerprints all over this. It’s clear to her that Lena is behind all of it, every bump in the road Lex is contending with. But Lex doesn’t seem capable of suspecting she could be the one outwitting him. He puts the blame instead on the people around him, and in the next month alone he exiles twelve people, none of whom are actually involved in the conspiracy.

    Kara understands, now, why Lena’s friends have been so close-lipped. Each of them knows only their own part in this machine. Lena isn’t giving any one person all the information, in case anyone is compromised or targeted by Lex’s constant suspicion, and her tactic is working. She’s protecting herself, just as Kara would want her to. Every hint of interference in Lex’s plans is like a message from Lena. I’m trying. Wait for me. Trust me. And Kara does.

    Her faith renewed, Kara turns her energy to the people of Athens.

    Rather than pouting her way through public appearances, Kara puts her effort into charming the crowds. She performs feats of strength and speed, helping with everything from moving marble for construction projects to performing as Athena herself in several theatre productions put on by Brainy, and with the help of Lena’s friends she keeps herself as visible as possible even within the confines of Lex’s requirements. She feels – she hopes – that this is what Lena would want. For Kara to take on the support that Lex is losing. It makes her feel productive rather than helpless, and even though she misses Lena every moment of every day, she keeps behaving.

    The nights are the loneliest. She spends most of them tossing and turning under her stifling lead dome, but the metal coffin does have one positive feature – while Clark is assigned to make sure she doesn’t leave it, he can’t see or hear through it any more than she can. It gives her some small haven of privacy, a privilege she misses dearly from her old life.

    Every step she makes, every conversation she has, holds the possibility of being watched. She gets only brief windows where she can speak frankly with anyone; usually it’s Kelly who lends her ear during the brief periods when Clark and Lex are otherwise occupied, and even then their walks in the gardens are always overshadowed by the potential of being overheard by a passing servant.

    “How are you holding up, Kara?” Kelly asks during one such walk, her face showing a keen understanding of just how much Kara has been suffering. “Really. I can’t imagine how difficult this has all been. All these secrets. We’re all only told what we need to know at any given time, but I’ve seen the way Lex has been working you to death.”

    “I know now that it’s necessary. I want to keep Lena safe more than anything,” Kara says, glancing around furtively. “It’s just very frustrating. I feel like I’m in stasis again. Just waiting for something to happen, not able to move things forward at all.”

    Kelly’s smile is sympathetic. “What do you usually do when you’re frustrated?”

    “Fly,” Kara sighs. She gazes up at the sky, blue and cloudless and entirely off-limits. Her ankles feel heavy, as if she’s held to the ground by great chains. Like the weight of the lead she’s wearing is pressing her into the earth. “When I was really upset I would fly out to sea. Or I’d just go up as far as I could, all the way into the clouds, until it got difficult to breathe. I’d feel the frost on my face and see the world around me. It always reminded me how small my problems were.”

    “I can see how that would put things in perspective,” Kelly says quietly. “I’m sorry you can’t do it now.”

    “Or I’d talk to my sister,” Kara continues. “She’s harsh sometimes, but she was always the logical one. She has a way of making things make sense. Cutting through the chaos.”

    “Is your sister…like you?”

    “What, with powers? No,” Kara says, chuckling a little. “She’s human, like you. She and her mother adopted me. She was the one who warned me that being a hero might not be everything I thought it was. And now here I am, dreaming about going back to the life I wanted out of for so long.”

    “You clearly have a calling for helping people,” Kelly notes. Kara shrugs indifferently.

    “I do. I just wish I could actually do that instead of...” Kara gestures around, her hands splayed. “This.”

    “After all this is over, will you want to keep being a hero? Playing the part, helping people in need?”

    “Maybe. If people really do need help. But what I want most is just…quiet,” Kara admits. Her mind wanders, as it has so often lately, to the bedroom she shared with Lena before all of this. Their little safe haven. She thinks of the property near Elis where she had just started to imagine fashioning them a place of their own someday, before everything fell apart. Of a house, and a garden, and maybe some animals. A kind of peace that seems unimaginable now. “To find a place where Lena and I can be together without any of this hanging over us anymore. Somewhere to pass our days slowly. A home.”

    Kelly looks almost as wistful as Kara feels. “That sounds lovely.”

    “Yeah.” Kara kicks at the ground, sending a tussock sailing through the air to land on one of Lex’s perfectly manicured stone walkways. Kelly’s presence is warm, but it doesn’t quite banish the chill of Lena’s absence.

    “If you could write to this sister of yours, would it bring you some peace?” Kelly asks. Kara frowns.

    “Wouldn’t that put her in danger?”

    “I would only give it to someone I absolutely trust to deliver.”

    “She doesn’t read much,” Kara says, chewing on her lip. “Lena taught her some before we left, but…would someone be able to read it to her?”

    “Of course.”

    The idea is a nice one, now that Kara considers it. Lena’s lessons were thorough, and Kara can write now in Greek almost as well as she can in Kryptonian – even if she might not get a reply, writing her thoughts down and knowing Alex has heard them might settle her mind.

    “Then, yes,” Kara says, smiling in earnest for what feels like the first time in an age. “At least to let her know I’m okay. I don’t think any of us expected me to be gone for this long.”

    Kelly returns the smile with one of her own. “That, at least, is something I can help with.”

    As the cool wintery weather starts to turn back to warm days Kelly manages to sneak Kara some papyrus and ink, and by night Kara writes a letter to Alex.

    I’ve been away a lot longer than I planned, Kara scribbles in the neat Greek characters Lena worked so hard to teach her, hunched over under her lead dome and using her own thigh as a table. She smiles a little when she imagines Alex reading those words with a muttered ‘I told you so’. But things got out of hand pretty quickly.

    It’s hard to explain the situation without revealing too much. Things are in flux here. But I’m trying to have faith. It’s hard, but I’m trying. I miss you and Eliza, and our house. I miss sitting on the roof drinking wine. I miss Lena’s bread and Eliza’s fish stew. I miss sunsets over the olive grove. But what I’m doing here will hopefully bring me back.

    Kara flexes her hand, chewing on the end of her reed-pen. She doesn’t want to panic them by mentioning what’s happened to Lena, but nor does she want to outright lie.

    I’m sorry I’m being vague. I don’t want either of you getting hurt. But I want you to know I’m safe right now, and we’re coming home as soon as we can. And, Alex…you were right. About fame, about everything I thought I wanted before Lena came into our lives. It’s all so much more than I bargained for. Once I’m home, I never want to think about godhood again.

    I love you both. Stay safe.

    She signs off with her name, hiding it in the folds of her nightclothes, and in the morning she hands it off to Kelly to be delivered. Kelly promises to entrust it to only her most discreet messenger.

     

     

    Her letter leaves the city undetected, and after weeks of perfect obedience Lex finally deigns to allow Kara to walk the grounds around the house freely rather than just marching through its halls like a caged lion. She’s still supervised at all times and required to wear her lead armour, but it helps to be able to take her pacing outdoors. It’s during one such restless walking session that she’s stopped by a dark-skinned soldier she’s never met, but who seems strangely familiar.

    “Kara, right?” the man says in a comforting baritone, taking off his crested helmet as he jogs to catch up to her. His smile is warm as he clasps her arm. “James. Kelly’s brother.”

    “Oh!” Kara says, shaking with slightly more enthusiasm now. That explains the resemblance. “Right, she said you were returning soon. Welcome back, I guess.”

    James chuckles. “It is nice to be back, though things have gotten much more tense since I left.”

    “That’s an understatement,” Kara grumbles. James tucks his helmet under his arm, matching his walking pace to Kara’s.

    “It might cheer you up to know I’ve gained permission from the tyrannos to let me train you a bit.”

    “Train me?”

    “I understand your abilities make swordplay a moot point, but I reminded Lex that Athena’s daughter should be able to at least feign some combat ability,” James explains, saluting a passing group of guards as they pass. They seem deferential to him; his rank must carry some weight, even if Lena claimed Lex doesn’t give him much credence. “Basic forms, battle strategies. If he wants you leading an army you should be prepared for warfare.”

    Kara’s skin crawls at the reminder of what Lex expects her to do.

    “I guess that’s true.”

    “Come on,” James says easily, inclining his head towards the street. “He’s given me leave to take you to the gymnasium.”

    The walk is surprisingly leisurely. James makes conversation while they pick their way through the streets, and Kara notes that James is sticking to the most highly populated areas even if it isn’t the most efficient route. He chats to merchants and other soldiers along the way, and stops every time some reverent or grateful passerby wants to talk to Kara. Encourages it, even. It takes them over an hour and a half to get to the training grounds where with Lex it had only taken twenty minutes, and after the first few citizen encounters Kara understands that James recognises the same thing she does. The importance of the act.

    Thankfully he doesn’t train her in the same area as the rest of the soldiers. He takes her to a private sparring ring, tossing her a short sword and a shield and leaning two spears against the stone wall.

    “Are we really here to train?” Kara asks, slipping the shield onto her arm. She’s never held one before but the leather straps seem self-explanatory, and James nods his approval.

    “We are,” James says. He swings the sword through the air in a quick series of movements. “But I’m also hoping the noise interference from the equipment will keep us from being overheard. You seem like you need someone to talk to.”

    Kara huffs a short laugh, raising her weapons. “I like Kelly, but I really don’t know you, James.”

    “I understand that. You don’t have to trust me,” James says, lowering his own sword to gently redirect Kara’s stance. “But Lena is my friend, too. She’s dear to all of us.”

    He nudges her feet apart, and Kara huffs. “I know.”

    James clearly feels her unease. He backs off, raising his sword and shield to match Kara.

    “Let’s just train.”

    Over the course of the day James shows Kara how to hold her shield and spear and how to swing a sword without looking like she’s never done it before. They drill a few forms, and Kara learns how to parry and disarm without hurting someone; all of it is tailored to take her strength into account, though once she’s gotten the basics down she knocks James into the dust so many times that by the afternoon they switch to theoretics. She memorizes the foundations of Athenian battle strategy, with a focus on how it can be disrupted by someone with enough knowledge. Knowledge he imparts to her freely.

    Even if the exercise doesn’t leave her exerted it’s still a wonderful way to occupy her mind, and by the time the sun sets and they’re due back at the house she finds herself more relaxed than she’s been since they arrived here.

    “Thank you,” Kara says as they carry the equipment back to the armory, trying her best to smile genuinely when James raises his eyebrows. “This was actually really great. And you’re right. I do need someone to talk to. If there’s a single person I think is genuinely trustworthy in Athens it’s probably your sister, so you must be pretty reliable too.”

    James chuckles. “I’m flattered.”

    Kara sighs, setting her spear in its place on the rack and handing the shield off to James to be hung. “I know what I have to do. And I’m doing it. But it’s getting harder and harder to believe it will ever end.”

    “I know,” James says, setting a large, comforting hand on her shoulder. “And I know telling you to take heart isn’t terribly helpful. But it will end. Lena is more capable than any of us know.”

    “That’s the one thing I am sure about,” Kara says.

    Training is added to her routine after their first successful session. Every few days when Lex’s schedule is too busy to allow for public appearances James collects her from the house and she gets a few precious hours of dropping the façade, and having a task to dedicate herself to – learning enough to become the warrior everyone expects her to be – helps to keep the creeping despair at bay.

    Kara keeps Lena’s note tucked into a pocket of the infernal armour Lex continues to make her wear, and every time he puts her on parade – which is increasingly often, as the people are now as likely to turn on him as to praise him if she isn’t there – it gives her strength. She makes appearances and opens festivals for Lex, she trains with James; as spring begins in earnest and the world around her starts to bloom again after a longer-than-average winter, marking almost a full year since she met Lena for the first time, Kara tries to keep hope in her heart.

     

     

    She’s been parted from Lena for over three full months by the time change comes.

    Kara is woken not to the usual bright discomfort of her lead dome, but to sight and sound. It’s still the darkest part of the night, and she glances at the place where Clark usually stands watch only to find it empty, the dome turned over and still rolling slowly over the flagstones. And someone is standing over her in the dark. A woman, Kara thinks - she can’t make out the details for a few long seconds, her eyes still adjusting, but her suspicion is confirmed when her liberator speaks.

    “Get up. Now.”

    “Wh – who are you?” Kara asks, swinging her legs out of bed and trying to orient herself to the situation. The woman remains terse.

    “A friend. Put these on.”

    A soft bundle is thrust at Kara’s chest – a dark hood and cloak, and a plain chiton. She frowns.

    “What’s going on? Is this some kind of trap to get me to try and escape? Because I’m not putting Lena in danger just to -”

    “Do you want to see her or not, Orpheus?” the woman snaps.

    Despite her aggressive tone, Kara’s distrust drops. She blinks the sleep from her eyes, focusing through the darkness, and finds that she recognizes the woman’s face at once.

    “You’re the one who gave me the note!”

    “And you almost blew my cover,” the woman says shortly. “I’m hoping you’ll be a little more subtle tonight.”

    “How was I supposed to know you were undercover?”

    “You weren’t, that’s the point. It was a risk, but Sam and I told Lena you were on the brink of doing something stupid if she didn’t give you something to hold onto,” the woman explains, making an impatient gesture until Kara starts to change into the outfit she was given. “I’m glad to see it worked.”

    “Sam? You’re both working with Lena?” Kara slips quickly into the chiton, pinning it crookedly and throwing the cloak over her shoulders. “Who are you?”

    The woman ignores her. She leads Kara to the door, peering out into the hall.

    “Use your powers to tell me if there are any patrols nearby,” the woman whispers, before she slips into the night like an assassin.

    Kara doesn’t have much choice but to follow. She trails the woman silently through the grounds, giving her nods or shakes of the head at every corner and slipping past Lex’s guards until they’re winding through the unoccupied side-streets of Athens in the dead of night, and the woman’s pace is so quick that Kara has to use a bit of superspeed to keep up.

    “Can’t we just fly there?” Kara asks, jogging a little and then grunting when the woman throws an arm into her chest. She pushes Kara into a shadowy corner, pressing a finger to her lips, and a few moments later a group of four armoured men marches down the street Kara had been about to step into.

    “Don’t be so predictable,” the woman says quietly, stepping back out when the group has rounded the corner. “You know they’re watching the skies.”

    “They’re watching the grounds, too. What if Kal hears us?”

    “He’s in his own lead box right now. Courtesy of Lillian.”

    Kara stops dead.

    “Lillian helped you?”

    “She’s under the impression that you and your cousin are both being transported out of the city,” the woman explains, grabbing Kara’s arm and pulling her – somewhat harshly – until she starts walking again. “She was eager enough to be rid of you that she undermined Lex. You did a fantastic job in making her hate you.”

    “Oh,” Kara says, blinking and following at the woman’s fast pace again. “So what’s going to happen to my cousin?”

    “He’s not actually leaving the city if that’s what you’re asking,” comes the irritated reply. “In the morning he’ll be found with some strategic evidence, and with you gone Lex will probably throw Lillian in prison for treason. You’ll have a short time before he realizes you’re still in Athens.”

    Kara frowns. As much as he’s been the primary force keeping her from Lena, she still believes in her heart that past whatever spell Lex has put him under, her cousin is still in there somewhere. Her blood. The only remaining link to Krypton. She didn’t know him before, but Lex had described him as principled when he first came to the city. Moral. He’d wanted to be a hero. If she can separate him from whatever Lex is using to control his mind, maybe that will still be waiting underneath.

    “He’s under Lex’s thrall,” the woman says, somewhat less harshly. Perhaps she senses Kara’s turmoil. “I know he’s your family, but there’s going to come a time where you need to do something about him.”

    “I can help him,” Kara says with more confidence than she truly feels. “I know I can.”

    The woman doesn’t retort. She leads Kara deeper into the city, sticking to dirty side streets that bring them closer to the outskirts than Kara has ever been. Lex avoids theses areas on his parades – he calls them the slums. Poor housing and warehouses, workshops and taverns. Not the people Lex cares about impressing. The journey ends at a building that stands out against the seedier area around it. It’s well-maintained on the outside, brightly painted and hung with flowers, but Kara’s perception changes when she reads the sign outside.

    “Lena is here? At a porneion?” she hisses. The woman rolls her eyes.

    “This is a house of hetaerae, not a brothel. And she’s not being made to work.”

    Of course. What had Sam said, at the symposium months ago? Mercy Graves is the queen of all hetaera who want to ply their trade in Athens. Mercy had been so smug in her conversation with Kara that night, and Sam had jumped in to divert Kara’s anger.

    She is a very dangerous enemy to make. If this is truly where Lena has been kept this whole time, Sam was right. If Kara had made a true enemy of Mercy it might have gotten Lena into trouble.

    “Go inside, and keep that cloak around you,” the woman says. “Someone will be waiting.”

    She’s already disappearing before she’s even finished speaking, but Kara calls after her. “Wait! What’s your name?”

    The woman looks likely to ignore her, but after some hesitation she answers, her full lips pursed.

    “Andrea.”

    “Why are you helping me, Andrea?”

    “I’m not,” Andrea says, stepping into the shadows for good. “I’m helping her.”

    Kara is left alone on the stoop with only the muffled sound of lyre music and voices behind the heavy wooden door, and after a deep and bracing breath Kara pushes it open.

    The interior is as nicely decorated as the outside, hung with woven tapestries much like Lex’s home, these ones in deep reds and pinks. It’s warm and fragrant – Kara can see flower petals have been thrown on the hearth to scent the smoke, and there are tiny decorative vases of perfumed oils diffusing on the table in the entryway. The music and voices are coming from a room to her left where warm light is spilling out of what sounds to be a small gathering of mixed genders, but a voice calls out from the right before Kara can go towards it.

    “See, I knew you wanted my services. I’ve been waiting for ages!”

    It’s Sam, leaning against the opposite wall with a playful grin. With the knowledge Kara now has, and the promise of seeing Lena again buoying her heart, Kara manages to laugh.

    “Maybe I would have come earlier if you’d asked before you kissed me.”

    “That would have nullified the entire point of the kiss, which was to stop you blabbing in front of Lex and Mercy,” Sam fires back with a snort. “You were one sentence away from blowing years of work.”

    “Work doing what, exactly?”

    “Convincing them I’m not a threat. And keeping my friendship with Lena under wraps.”

    Lena. Just hearing her love’s name said by someone who isn’t an immediate threat is a balm, and Kara’s heart picks up speed.

    “Is she…” Kara swallows, wetting her suddenly dry lips. “Is she really here?”

    A haughty voice comes from behind Sam, attached to a formidable-looking woman Kara doesn’t recognize.

    “Why else do you think Andrea brought you here?”

    The relief of having something closer to a straight answer is immediate, but the shorter figure who steps out from the hallway behind Sam isn’t the person Kara really wants to see. She’s much smaller than Lena, a compact blonde woman about Lillian’s age with a discerning expression as she takes Kara in head to toe much like Mercy once did. Sam defers to her immediately. She radiates authority, and even in her later years in comparison to the other hetaerae Kara has met she can see how this woman might have had a very successful career.

    “The last few months have taught me to assume everyone has ulterior motives,” Kara says carefully. The woman smiles.

    “Good. An important lesson to learn.” She holds out her hand, and Kara clasps it. “Catherine Grant. Former proprietress of this establishment, before it was unceremoniously taken from me.”

    “Nice to meet you,” Kara says glancing around the room again. She can see some stairs leading to an upper level, and she wonders if that’s where Lena is waiting. It’s difficult not to just bolt up there to check when the thing she’s been waiting on for months is so close. It’s hard to think of anything else besides that this might all be over. “What’s your stake in all this? Everyone seems to have a deep-seated reason to work against Lex.”

    “I want my business back.”

    Kara almost laughs. But Cat is dead serious, and Sam elaborates.

    “Lex took possession through a loophole and gave this place to Mercy, along with all the others in the city,” Sam explains. “Let the neighbourhood around it fall to ruin. Started treating the hetaerae like slaves. We all want them both out.”

    “And do we need to worry about Mercy turning up here?” Kara asks, glancing at the room where party sounds are still audible. Cat is unbothered.

    “She’s in Corinth. Sent by Lex to try to sway Morgan Edge back to his side, after the marriage with Lena fell through. His allegiance has been wavering. But we have an agent tasked with making sure she fails.”

    “Great,” Kara says, only half-paying attention. It’s been so long that she can’t be sure, but she’s almost certain she can hear Lena’s heartbeat again. “Look, I don’t mean to be rude. But is Lena actually here? Because if I could just see her, I think -”

    “Kara?”

    Oh.

    Something ragged and broken inside Kara seals itself back together for the first time since they were parted.
