    “Protect the citizens!”

    Lex’s troops hesitate in the face of armed resistance. The two groups face each other with weapons raised in a stalemate, one side protecting Lex and the other protecting the people he claims to rule, and only when Lex shouts another furious command does his unit finally charge.

    “Take care of Lex!” J’onn shouts to Kara, ducking one spear and parrying another as the two sides meet in a symphony of metal on metal. “They’ll surrender if he does!”

    Leaving the sword combat in their capable hands, Kara flies to join Lena on the podium instead. The circle Kara burned around her is still smoking, and now that his protectors are engaged Lena steps over it to approach him like one might approach a rabid wolf in the wild. Cautious, but pushed forward by the need to rid the world of the danger he poses.

    “Just give up, Lex,” Lena says, raising her voice over the battle-din. “Nobody else needs to get hurt.”

    Lex laughs. It’s a manic sort of sound, with a smile that doesn’t reach his eyes. “You think I’ll surrender that easily? You have no idea what I’ve done to bring Greece together.”

    “What you’re doing is splintering it,” Kara argues, a half-step behind Lena. “You’re burning everything down for a threat that isn’t even here yet! Stop this madness!”

    “My allies will be here soon. I’d rather this city reduced to ashes than not be in my hands,” Lex snarls.

    At that, Lena smiles. She and Lex are a spear’s length from each other now, Kara just behind while Lena takes the lead, and at such a close distance for the first time Kara can see a sliver of fear in Lex’s face when Lena reacts with mirth rather than despair.

    “Oh, you mean Mercy’s little mission to sway Morgan Edge?” Lena says confidently, tilting her head with the same condescending tone Kara has seen Lex use so many times to make Lena feel small. “He’s not coming.”

    The colour bleeds from Lex’s face.

    “I kept him updated on the situation the whole time you had me captive,” Lena explains, finally revealing to him the months of work she’s been laying out to Kara the last few days. “It turns out that after you paraded Kara around as Athena’s daughter, he had no interest in going against the will of the gods to help you fight her. And neither did any of your other conquered vassals.”

    “You…” Lex says, his face twisted in disbelief. “You. Of course. My clever sister.” There’s no pride in his voice – just vicious hatred.

    “I told you you were underestimating her,” Kara says. Lex doesn’t spare her a glance.

    “And I knew I should have just killed her when you arrived and been done with it.”

    “Funny how that works,” Lena says, unfazed while Kara barely resists the urge to squash him like a bug. “I believe they call it hubris.”

    It becomes clear as Lex struggles to reply that the skirmish around them isn’t going in his favour. J’onn’s fighters have superior numbers, and Lex’s small remaining force is falling back to the platform to group around Lex with their spears fanned out against the advance. They look to him for instructions, but he hardly acknowledges their presence. Instead his attention moves to Kara.

    “You won’t kill me. And I will never stop,” he says in a deadly hiss, his teeth bared like an animal. “For the rest of your days you’ll sleep with one eye open, because I could be on the other side of that door waiting. Just waiting to take her away from you again.”

    Fury surges through Kara with such intensity that she charges forward, past Lena and across the platform to grab him by the neck, lifting him bodily into the air with eyes glowing. Her grasp isn’t as tight as it could be, but still he reacts as if she’s cut off his air supply – he makes pathetic choking sounds as his hands scrabble at her wrist, his legs twisting and flailing. His eyes are so wide that they almost protrude, and Kara can feel his fluttering heartbeat through the arteries under her fingers. How easy it would be to simply squeeze until the threat is eliminated and Lena is safe. And he, for the first time, seems to understand just how easily she could do so.

    “Never again,” Kara growls, holding him at eye level so that she can speak low and close. The searing glow of her eyes lights up the terror on his face. “Do you understand me?”

    Lex’s confidence finally cracks.

    “Do something!” Lex gurgles at the soldiers still grouped around them, struggling to speak past Kara’s hold on his throat. His movement does nothing but make it more difficult for him to breathe, and yet he continues his fruitless wriggling. But the reminder of Kara’s strength seems to have made their courage fail. One makes a halfhearted stab at her, only for his spear to glance off her arm like a twig against stone.

    “We are mortals, sir!” one of them says, his eyes wide as plates. “We can’t fight a god!”

    “You think I care?” Lex hisses, his foot making contact with Kara’s knee but not moving it an inch. “Just distract her, damn it!”

    None of them step forward this time. They all look, instead, to Kara.

    “Is this the man you want to follow?” Kara says, lifting him up high. His face is starting to redden, but she can still feel air and blood moving under her palm. Her hold is superficial. He isn’t in true danger yet – but he doesn’t need to know that. “One who happily sacrifices your lives for his own? He’s a coward.”

    While Lex wrestles with his mortality, his men throw their weapons down one by one.

    Kara tosses him to the ground in a heap when the last spear falls. He gasps for air, scrambling away from her only to find himself surrounded by discarded weapons and a disbanded honour guard.

    “It’s over,” Lena says. Lex grabs for the nearest spear, a desperate final attempt to stave them off, but when his eyes land on something in the sky his fear turns to savage glee.

    “Not yet.”

    Before Kara can so much as turn to follow his eyeline, she’s hit from behind by a force she didn’t think was possible. It knocks the breath from her, slamming her into the ground and holding her there, and for a few confusing moments she struggles to get air into her lungs – everything is pain, unfamiliar and heavy, making her limbs slow to respond -

    “Kara!”

    The fear in Lena’s voice is primal. It centres Kara, brings her back to reality, and with a great heave she manages to throw off the weight holding her down. Her elbow hits flesh hard enough to crush bones, but the solid body it glances off instead can be only one person.

    She twists in Clark’s grip, wrapping her legs around his midsection in a wrestling move that James showed her and using the leverage to flip their positions; and clasping her hands together, she brings them down hard on the centre of his sternum.

    It’s clear that Clark is as unaccustomed to pain as she is. The hit caves in his decorative chest-guard, winding him enough to loosen his grip, and Kara manages to launch herself off his body and fly straight up into the dusky sky. She can’t hear Lena or Lex anymore. She can’t hear much of anything besides her own heart and Clark’s winded breathing – she lost her sword and shield in the struggle, and when Clark launches himself after her it’s all she can do to out-maneuver him in the air long enough to face him properly.

    “I don’t want to fight you!” Kara yells, giving one final appeal to her cousin as they hover over the city in a mid-air standoff. Clark’s red cloak ripples in the wind, torn almost in half during their tussle. “Kal-El, please. Don’t make me do this.”

    He flies at her like an arrow.

    The force of his impact sends them both reeling through the air. The world is spinning as they fall together, his fists making impact blindly even as Kara tries to pull away, but despite her efforts he drives them both into the ground of the city below, their impact not quite softened by the leather workshop whose roof they utterly destroy. Bits of stone and wood and terracotta tile rain down, burying them both, and Kara has a brief moment to be thankful that it seemed to be unoccupied. Only in the confusion of their landing does she manage to twist out of his range and land a hit of her own.

    Kara has never once, in all her years on this planet, had the experience of not holding back. Every moment of her time on Earth has been spent calculating the exact force she can put behind each movement. Everything from hugs to handshakes to simply walking is a math formula in her head. It’s become second-nature, not quite as natural as breathing but an everyday feature of her life nonetheless, and she’s never had the opportunity or inclination to stop.

    In this moment, she puts every ounce of her power behind her fist. She jumps into it, aiming her angle downwards and using the weight of her body to smash directly into his cheekbone, and Clark is knocked down so hard that he crashes through the adjacent wall, through the next building, and out into the street. His angled trajectory leaves a long, jagged scar in the ground, earth and stone parting like water to accommodate the force of his propelled body; what’s left of the building crumbles around Kara, sending their observers running,

    For a moment Clark is still. Kara takes stock of her body – she’s in pain, a chorus of dull throbbing where Clark’s fists impacted, but nothing feels broken. By the time Clark heaves himself back to standing Kara is ready for another attack. He rips his helmet off – it’s been molded to the shape of his face with a deep imprint of Kara’s fist, and he has to tear the metal to free himself from it – and throws it, his face is red and swollen beneath. His eyes begin to glow, and Kara braces herself. But the attack never comes.

    Clark turns his powers, instead, to a two-story home on the street corner.

    “No!” Kara shouts, but it’s too late. Clark has let loose his heat vision to sear a burning line through the home, immolating the wood supports and setting a blaze that continues to burn even when he moves on to the next building, this one a bakery whose owner Kara can see huddling in a corner of the shop floor. Clark is going for collateral damage rather than an honourable fight; before he can do more than property damage, Kara abandons her last hope of talking him out of this madness. She rams into him shoulder-first, slamming him away and cutting off his heat vision with her own body. It sears through the fabric over her arm, leaving a painful abrasion underneath that’s slow to heal in the moonlight.

    It doesn’t seem to matter how hard she hits him. He’s more worn down every time they’re hand-to-hand, losing stamina and slower to recover after every bout, but he’s completely unpredictable. Kara can never know if he’s going to attack, or destroy. He throws the disassembled parts of buildings they’ve ravaged in their fight into the streets, uses his heat vision to start fires that Kara has to scramble to put out, sends his frost breath sweeping over crowds. He’s on a rampage. Bloody and bruised, his armour dented and broken, he tests her at every turn.

    Soon the city is glowing in the encroaching dark. Buildings are ablaze, still others reduced to rubble, and Kara has no idea where Lena is.

    Kara herself isn’t in much better shape than Clark is. Even her superhuman resistance apparently can’t hold up forever against another solar-powered being - her ribs feel bruised, there’s a twinge in her knee every time she puts weight on it, and her lip is cracked open in two places. She spits blood onto the cobblestones, flexing the broken skin over her knuckles, as Clark pulls himself out of a pile of wreckage yet again.

    He’s never going to give in. In his current state, Kara realizes as he glares at her through the omnipresent orange glow, he’ll fight to the death. And he’s so weakened by the kryptonite that death might actually be a possibility.

    His chestplate has finally been torn off completely, leaving him in the ripped red tunic beneath, and through a gap in the collar Kara sees a glint of silver.

    It’s a pendant, Kara suddenly remembers. Their family crest. He’d been wearing it when they met in that horrible dungeon. She’d been preoccupied at the time, and had hardly given it a second thought. But it’s made of silver. And this time, as she watches, painful lines spread from the skin around it and up Clark’s neck to his face. The place it occupies on his chest is like an infection, red and enflamed.

    Enough.

    When Kara flies at him this time, she doesn’t go for a hit. She feints instead, grabbing him in a wrestling hold and flying straight upwards. He struggles against her, but he’s so beaten that she’s 150 feet in the air before he even gets an elbow in her side, and she’s so focused on ending this that she fights through the pain. She goes up, up, 200 feet and then 300 and then 5, and when she reaches the apex she twists them both and hurtles back down towards the acropolis at top speed.

    The impact of slamming him into the ground leaves a massive crater right in front of the Temple of Athena Polias, with Clark’s limp body at the bottom. Kara’s hands are scraped and she aches all over, but finally, Clark ceases his struggle and falls unconscious. His heart is still beating, but he doesn’t rise again.

    “I’m sorry,” Kara says, breathing the Kryptonian words in short, ragged gasps as the adrenaline eases off and the full breadth of physical pain washes over her. She reaches past his tunic to grab at the pendant, snapping the chain and pulling it away. “Forgive me.”

    It seems so small in her palm. The reflective El crest glints in the moonlight, tinged with orange, the chain spooled around it and hanging over her fingers. It’s sort of…beautiful, in a strange way. Mesmerizing. It makes her dizzy. She closes her hand around it, squeezing, but her grip feels weaker than it ever has before – an anxious sort of paranoia settles in her gut. Confusion and dread. Anger, with no source. Her hand shakes, and a web of silver lines crawls over the back of it and up her arm.

    It takes almost more energy than she can muster, but before the kryptonite can get further than her elbow Kara winds her arm back and pitches the pendant into space.

    The relief is palpable from the moment it leaves her hand, but even from the brief seconds she was touching it the sensation it brought on lingers in her blood. It leaves her exhausted like she’s never been before. Everything feels fuzzy, now – she staggers up and out of the crater with a monumental effort, squinting around the shadowy buildings around her, and only just manages to take flight and head back to the Pnyx. Her feet are dragging in the dirt before she arrives, and she can tell before she even lands that Lex and Lena aren’t there. She can’t hear either of their voices over the din of the city. She falls to her knees on the broken platform, trying to take full breaths through sore ribs.

    “Focus,” Kara whispers to herself. She clenches her fist, trying to ground herself with the pain. Her wounds are healing but the kryptonite seems to have slowed it down. “They have to be somewhere.”

    Shaking her head to clear the cobwebs clinging to her mind, Kara tries again.

    It’s difficult to focus over the chaos. She filters through all of it – the shouts of people putting out fires down below, families gathering to assess the damage done to their homes. She can hear James giving orders to dig out ruined buildings and J’onn gathering magistrates near the marketplace. But she hears other things, too, whispers that snake through everything else. Sounds she knows can’t be real. The sound of her favourite music box on Krypton. The mechanical voice of her pod in space, telling her there was an error in transit. The voices of her parents, telling her to be strong as they put her into it.

    It has to be the aftereffects of the kryptonite. But it still pulls her focus away from where it needs to be. When she does finally find Lena’s voice, it’s fuzzy and distant. As if heard through some kind of interference. She can barely hear the words, and she can’t for the life of her tell where they’re coming from.

    “You’d really kill me, Lex?”

    Kara’s heart drops. The world spins even more dramatically than it did when she was touching the kryptonite – the words sound like they’re coming from every direction, muffled but somehow also echoing.

    “Kill you? Oh, no. You were just an unfortunate loss in the siege of my city,” Lex says. He’s muffled, too - she hears the metallic scrape of iron dragging over stone and realizes, with a sudden lurch, that her sword isn’t in its sheath at her hip. “I’ll mourn your passing, and then direct the people’s ire towards the culprit – their precious demigod. Why, this is even her weapon. They’ll find it buried in your gut, and your loving brother grieving over your body.”

    “You’re insane.”

    Kara can hear them, but no matter how hard she focuses she can’t tune into their location. The desperation is humming in her ears, her reflexes feeling sluggish. Is this real? Or is it a conjuration of whatever horrible effect infuses the kryptonite? She has no idea. The panic gives her enough energy to fly again, looping around the city and listening hard, but she’s slower than she needs to be. It’s hard to stay in the air.

    “Unfortunate that it came to this, but you shouldn’t have thought you could outwit me,” Lex hisses. It sounds louder now, at least, and Kara flies lower.

    “I did outwit you. And it kills you, doesn’t it?” Lena says. She’s doing a good job covering up the terror in her voice, but Kara can still hear it, and it makes desperation claw at her chest. She has to find her now. “Not being the smartest person in the room?”

    “I am the most powerful man in Greece!” Lex shouts. “I rule the most populous city, I’ve created an army most men could only dream about – I am bringing us into the future! You are nothing!”

    “You’re pathetic,” Lena says derisively. “A tiny, insignificant man.”

    Wood screeches across a stone floor, and something shatters – pottery, falling from a table perhaps? The noise is sharp enough that it pulls Kara’s attention eastwards. Towards the Luthor estate.

    Where Lex has a lead-lined study.

    Kara cuts through the air cleanly, exhaustion forgotten. Landing hard in the gardens and squinting past the outer walls, she can finally see the source of her muted hearing - there’s just the slightest gap in the lining of Lex’s study. A crack near the door. When she focuses on it, her hearing sharpens.

    “Goodbye, sister. Say hello to your dear mother for me.”

    A blade hits flesh.

    Kara has already burst through the stone wall and torn through the thin lead veneer before she hears the wheeze of air leaving lungs. Her heart is in her throat, every fiber of her being seized in terror, praying to a god she’s not sure she truly believes in anymore that she’s made it in time –

    She finds them both on their knees in the study when the dust clears, facing each other. Lex is half-slumped over; there’s a gash in the fabric across Lena’s belly, and in her hand – buried deep in Lex’s chest - is the knife Alex made for her. Kara’s sword is lying on the ground nearby. The blade is kissed by blood.

    Lena drops her hand when she sees Kara. Lex falls back, wheezing, the knife staying in place just under his left shoulder.

    “Lena!” Kara gasps. She falls to her knees, pressing her hands to Lena’s belly and feeling her stomach churn when they come away red. The fabric is starting to soak with it, crimson crawling over white. “Rao, please, are you – is it -”

    “I’m fine,” Lena says, grabbing Kara’s wrists. “It’s okay, Kara. It’s not deep.”

    Lena’s voice is remarkably steady, though her hands are shaking. Her reassurance doesn’t stop Kara from assessing the wound herself. Only after a thorough look does Kara determine that it does look worse than it is - it’s only a superficial cut, slicing skin enough to draw an alarming amount of blood but not slicing deep enough to do any serious damage, and she unfastens the tattered remains of her cloak to press against it and slow the bleeding before she even takes a moment to look at Lex.

    An even darker crimson stain is creeping from the knife’s handle across his white robe, turning the purple sash burgundy.

    “Oh, brava,” Lex pants, looking down at the wound. An x-ray shows that Lena missed his heart, burying the knife just under his shoulder, but might have scraped a lung – but he doesn’t know that. The threat of more blood loss is enough to keep him down. “I underestimated you. Perhaps you really are a Luthor.”

    Lena squeezes Kara’s hands. Her blood is slowly drying on both of their palms. Her green eyes flash in Lex’s direction, and when she replies her voice is low and self-assured to contrast the pained shakiness of his.

    “I am nothing like you.”

    Likely drawn by the commotion of Kara smashing in half of the estate’s east wing, Kara can hear a group of people making their way through the halls towards the study. She helps Lena to her feet, the bleeding around her midsection slowing already helped by the pressure of Kara’s cloak, and Kara has time to pick up and clean her sword before Brainy and J’onn burst through the door trailed by a strange mixture of friends. Some are the sort of citizens Kara used to interact with on the street, ordinary bakers and merchants. Some look to be farmers, armed with pitchforks and scythes. And still others are the uncomfortable-looking, richly-dressed men Kara vaguely recognizes from Lex’s symposiums. Otis Graves is even among them, his face turning a sickly white when he sees Lex’s state.

    Lex attempts to pull himself to his feet, using his heavy desk for support, but the pain makes him lose his grip. He hits the ground again, sweating and swearing under his breath.

    “Help me,” Lex says through gritted teeth. He looks smaller than he ever has, huddled on the floor in a pool of his own blood with his supposed allies and subjects around him and none willing to obey. Even Otis, his most loyal follower, turns his eyes away as Lex’s power slips from his grasp.

    “We’ve put it to a vote,” Brainy says, giving Lex a small smile. “We’re creating an Assembly. And you are no longer welcome here.”

    Lex lunges at Brainy, but with one shoulder disabled only manages to seize at the hem of his robes before he’s kicked away like a rabid dog. “You can’t exile me. This is my city!”

    J’onn smiles down at him with the satisfaction of a man whose family is finally avenged. “Not anymore.”

    Lex’s breathing is coming quicker, now. An animal backed into a corner. Lena kneels next to him and he flinches away, baring his teeth.

    “Don’t touch me!”

    Lena doesn’t so much as pause. She grabs the handle of the knife and pulls it out of his shoulder with no remorse, her face set in grim determination, and presses a shred of Kara’s cloak into the bleeding wound. Significantly harder than necessary, perhaps, but it does staunch the blood flow even if Lex howls in pain.

    “Take him away, please,” Lena says, wiping the knife and then her hands on the skirt of her peplos. “He can be exiled or executed if he survives. The Assembly will decide.”

    There’s a general grumble of discontent over that. None seem enthused about ensuring his survival, but a few people step forward when Lena raises an impatient brow. “I have no need for him to be transported comfortably.”

    The citizens seem happier at that. Three of them drag him away by the arms while his affluent former friends follow, and his composure crumbles with pain and humiliation as he’s hauled from the room.

    Lena collapses into Kara’s arms the moment they’re alone. The study is open to the air now, and the moonlight filtering through the ruined walls combined with Lena’s safe proximity is making Kara’s wounds feel better by the minute.

    “I thought you were…” Kara’s voice breaks, and she pulls Lena tighter to bury her face in her dark, fragrant hair. “I could barely hear you, and I thought he might have -”

    “I needed to lead him somewhere Clark couldn’t find us,” Lena explains. She sounds about as exhausted as Kara feels. “Take advantage of his distraction. With Clark as his bodyguard, if I had waited…”

    Kara nods tightly. “You’re right. It was smart to bring him here.”

    “He needed to be stopped. He came at me with your sword, and I – I just -” It’s Lena’s voice that breaks this time. Kara pulls back enough to tuck Lena’s hair behind her ear, taking Lena’s face gently in her hands.

    “You did what needed to be done,” Kara says firmly. “You protected yourself. You protected everyone.” She kisses Lena’s forehead, mindless of the dust coating her skin, trailing down over the slope of her nose. “You did it, Lena.”

    “Do you think he’ll live?” Lena’s voice is smaller than before. Almost ashamed to be asking the question. Kara isn’t sure if it’s shame over her part in his injury, or shame over possibly hoping he survives. Over still holding on to the affection he once held as her brother. Perhaps it’s both.

    “If the wound doesn’t get infected? Maybe. It’s hard to say. You missed his heart.”

    “I honestly don’t know if that was intentional or not,” Lena says drily. Kara chuckles.

    “Want me to fry him for good measure?”

    Lena tucks her head into Kara’s shoulder, winding her arms tight around Kara’s middle. “Maybe later.”

    It’s a while before they disentangle from each other and leave the crumbling estate behind. The street outside is raucous, not with conflict now but with celebration, and Lena is gazing at the front of the house with distant eyes taking what Kara truly hopes is her last look at the building that was both of their prisons when Kara hears a voice both familiar and not.

    “Kara Zor-El?”

    Kara turns.

    Clark is standing in the street behind them. His voice is softer than Kara has ever heard it - it’s lost the edge of anger she’s gotten so used to, gained a gentleness that reminds Kara painfully of her own father. He’s haggard and beaten, but his eyes are clear. Shiny with tears.

    A spark of hope lights in Kara’s chest.

    “Kal?” Kara says, cautious still. She shifts to keep Lena slightly behind her, still holding tight to Lena’s hand, but he doesn’t seem interested in fighting anymore. “How do you feel?”

    “Like I’ve been having an endless nightmare,” he says. His heavy brows knit together. “It’s still…hard to tell what’s real and what isn’t.”

    Kara can’t imagine what the inside of his mind must be like. She had only touched the kryptonite for a few seconds, and still it lingered like a bitter taste on the tongue – Clark had it on his person for months.

    “I know. Lex had you brainwashed,” Kara explains. She takes a single step forward, and he doesn’t attack or shrink away. “But the stone is gone. You’re safe now.”

    “I thought…” A tear streaks down Clark’s face, leaving a clean line through the soot and dust. His voice trembles. “I thought my family was dead. All the information in my pod said that Krypton left no survivors. How are you here? Are you even real?”

    The emotion in his voice is enough for Kara to put aside her caution. She’s never heard anything behind his words besides fury or emptiness. If he’s capable of feeling again, of seeing reason, maybe he truly is free.

    Maybe she truly has her cousin back.

    “I’m the only one left,” Kara says softly. “We’re the only ones left. My parents put me in a pod, too. Just like you.”

    Clark takes a shaky breath. “I don’t remember you. Your name is in my pod’s family records, but…”

    “You were a baby. I was supposed to protect you, but I…I’m sorry.” Kara’s voice breaks. “I’m sorry I never found you.”

    “Sorry?” Clark says, cracking the first smile she’s ever seen on his face. It’s small, but genuine. “You saved me from Lex’s enslavement. You’ve given me my life back. My family.”

    Lena’s hand squeezes in Kara’s before releasing, pushing her slightly toward him, and it gives Kara enough strength to stride forward and pull Clark into a crushing hug. He wraps his arms tight around her torso, shaking slightly with withheld emotion, and it’s the first time since Kara landed on Earth that she doesn’t have to hold back her affection for fear of bodily harm. A sob escapes her, seizing in her chest, before she can hold it back.

    “Will you teach me about Krypton?” Clark asks in an unsteady voice. “Our family? The archive in my pod taught me some. But it all seemed so foreign. I never got the hang of the language. I want to learn about our people.”

    Kara smiles. Clark releases her and she reaches back for Lena, lacing their fingers together and setting her free hand on Clark’s broad shoulder.

    “I’ll teach you whatever you want. But I think we have some work to do here, first.”

    A new section of the manor’s broken roof caves in right on cue, kicking up a wave of dust and pebbles that sweeps out onto the street and over their sandals. Clark winces.

    “I wanted to be a hero and I ended up destroying half the city,” he says heavily. “I don’t think anyone here is ever going to trust me again.”

    “I think they’ll be much more forgiving if you help to rebuild it,” Lena suggests. “It would speed the process up considerably.”

    Clark nods. “That’s the least I can do. Where will you two go, after all this? Are you staying in Athens?”

    “For a while. We’ll help get things in place here,” Kara says. Lena nods in agreement.

    “But, once that’s done…we have some rebuilding to do for ourselves,” Lena says, squeezing Kara’s hand encouragingly.

    “Where?”

    Lena smiles, and joy warms Kara like a sunburst when she realizes what Lena means. Rebuilding. The life that had seemed far out of reach from the tiny room they planned it in, but now could actually be possible. Lena wants it, too.

    “Elis.”

     

     

     

     

    The sea is warm and clear when Kara dives in from the air. It’s a beautiful aquamarine against the blue sky just starting to tinge with the pink of early evening, the light filtering through the water even as she cuts a clean line towards the sandy bottom with a linen bag in hand. A school of glittering fish scatters as she swims through them, revealing a large and slow-moving sea turtle making its steady way down for food just like she is; Kara stops to drift underneath it for a moment, letting the current carry them both further out to sea. It peers curiously down at her and she peers back, releasing a slow stream of bubbles up to the surface before twisting around and diving down with a hard kick.

    The sea floor here is mostly sandy, but through the clear water she spots her prize. A wide bed of fat mussels are clustered around a seaweed-dotted rocky outcropping, and Kara opens her bag to scoop up a decent portion. It churns up silt and weeds, making the clear water murky, but a quick check assures Kara her work here is done anyways – she has plenty. She kicks off from the bottom, zipping past the turtle again and heaving a big breath of fresh air when she finally surfaces.

    The waves are calm today, so rather than going to shore Kara hovers on the surface of the water to scrub the barnacles and grit from the outsides before she soars back into the sky and heads for home. She slows her flight slightly, not rushed and worried about being seen but unhurried and easy, letting her hair dry in the wind with the bag tucked under her arm.

    She lands just outside the gate, pushing through it – it squeaks, and Kara makes a mental note to take care of it in the morning – to head towards the house. Its sturdy stone walls are familiar now, the foundations still strong but shored up by Kara’s hands, and the smell of slow-baking bread wafts from the open windows. She wanders past the fence that separates them from the neighbouring quince orchard, buzzing in the spring warmth, and their little pasture full of goats and chickens.

    She hears Lena humming before she sees her crouched among the rows of their lush garden, picking herbs.

    Lena doesn’t hear her approach. Kara takes the opportunity to stop and take in the view she’s missed despite having been gone for less than a day; Lena is barefoot, humming a simple and as ever slightly off-key melody while she snips a sprig of thyme with a knife and smells it, smiling at the fresh scent and adding it to her already over-full basket. The breeze ruffles her dark hair, held in place by a wide-brimmed hat, and the layers of her light linen peplos. She’s so beautiful that it makes Kara’s chest ache with perfect happiness.

    It's a feeling she’s grown used to, though it never gets less lovely. For close to a year now they’ve been living here on Lena’s old property, at first flying back and forth from Athens while Kara fixed it up and then moving in for good to work on making a home together, and in that year they’ve settled into a kind of harmony Kara never imagined herself having. Lena has continued to offer scribing to the nearby villages and has taken much of what Eliza taught her to offer herbs and medicines, while Kara spends most of her time taking care of the house and property and doing occasional labour jobs. Clark decided to stay in Athens to facilitate the new government, but once things had stabilized Lena hadn’t wanted anything to do with it anymore. And Kara had shared that sentiment. She still visits often to see Clark, though; it’s there that she’s returning from now, in fact. But Kara never spends more than a few hours from home.

    There’s privacy here. Solace. Peace. They still visit the old house to have supper with Alex, Kelly, and Eliza every week or so, and travelling to new places together is still something Kara loves, but most of their time is spent here. Together.

    When she finally notices Kara’s presence Lena’s smile gets wide and dimpled, and she takes off her hat to run her fingers through her long hair.

    “Kara! There you are. I thought you were going to be home at midday?”

    “I made a stop along the way,” Kara says, offering the still-dripping bag like a tribute. Lena peers in, and her face brightens.

    “You didn’t have to do that, darling.”

    “They’re your favourite,” Kara shrugs. “I thought we could cook them in wine.”

    Lena presses a quick kiss to her cheek, untangling Kara’s windblown hair with a practiced hand. “Is there a special occasion I’m forgetting?”

    “Nothing terribly special. Spring always makes me think of you.”

    “I thought I was Eurydice, not Persephone,” Lena drawls. It pulls a chuckle out of Kara, and she tries to put into words the thought that diverted her trip home.

    “You are. But…spring is when we first met. When you changed my life,” Kara says, setting the bag down on the grass so she can more easily loop her arms around Lena’s waist. She’s slightly hampered by the basket on Lena’s hip, but she makes do. “And it was when we were reunited, after everything. Now it’s our third spring, in a home that’s just ours, and I’m so happy that it hardly feels real.”

    Lena’s whole face softens. She sets her basket down next to the bag of mussels, and with a hand looped around the back of Kara’s neck she guides her down for a heartfelt kiss.

    “So am I, love.”

    Lena’s basket is full of herbs and the first spring vegetables, and with Lena’s fresh bread, some wine and garlic, and the mussels Kara gathered it should make for a perfect supper. It’s a quiet life, a slow pace that allows Kara to enjoy each moment as it comes - sweet days spent seeing new sights, or simply enjoying each other’s company in their home. Maybe in the future when they’ve had their fill of enjoying solitude together that might change. She could help people the way that Clark does, on her own terms. But not yet. Not now.

    For now, after everything they went through to get here, Kara wouldn’t trade this for all the fame and noble purpose the world could offer.

     

    Chapter End Notes

    I DID IT!!! This fic has haunted me for YEARS, and finally it's out of my brain and exists in the world. Writing it was a blast, and hopefully you enjoyed it just as much!
